We observe that the case
\alpha=\infty
does not use of the size of
{\mathcal{L}}_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}}
, and only requires a single vertex in it, which is intuitive considering the threshold nature of
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
).
It remains to prove Proposition
4.8
, which is the content of the following subsection.

4.2.
The algorithm producing the cover expansion

Now we give the algorithm producing the expanded cover of any discrete set
{\mathcal{L}}
without a proper cover, thence, proving the desired proposition.

Setup for the algorithm.

Recall the notation from Definitions
4.4
and
4.7
. Throughout, we will assume that
{\mathcal{L}}
does not admit a proper cover in Definition
4.6
and that
(B_{z_{i}}:i\in[m])
are the cells satisfying (
4.5
).
Contrary to Definition
4.7
, which allocates the initial cells
B_{z_{i}}

to boxes

B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}}
, the algorithm allocates the labels
i
,
i\leq m
of the initial cells
B_{z_{i}}

towards each other
in discrete rounds
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
We write
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ j
to indicate that label
i
is allocated to label
j
in the allocation of round
r
. We also write

{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}:=\{(i,j):i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ j\}_{i\leq m};\quad\mathrm{Cells}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}:=\bigcup_{i\leq m}\big\{i:i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ j\big\};\quad{\mathcal{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}:=\{j:\mathrm{Cells}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\neq\emptyset\}.

In each round
r\geq 0
, the boxes
\{B_{j}^{{}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}}\}_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
, and the centers of these boxes are completely determined by
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}
by the formula

(4.15)

B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}:=\Lambda\bigg(z_{j},\frac{1}{{\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}}\sum_{i\in\mathrm{Cells}_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}\ell_{i}\bigg)\quad\emph{for}\quad j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}};

where
\Lambda(x,s)
is a box of volume
s
centered at
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
(see (
2.1
)).
Since label
j
corresponds to center
z_{j}
across different rounds, by slightly abusing notation we also write
B_{z_{i}}\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
if and only if
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ j
.
We say that
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}
satisfies one (or more) conditions in Definition
4.7
if
(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}}
with allocation
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}
satisfies the condition(s).

The algorithm starts with the identity as initial allocation
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{0}}}
that induces possibly overlapping boxes
B_{1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\dots,B_{m}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
; we will show that
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{0}}}
already satisfies (near) and (vol.) of Definition
4.7
.
In each stage the algorithm attempts to remove an overlap – a non-empty intersection – between a pair of boxes by re-allocating a few cell labels, while maintaining properties (near) and (vol.); we achieve (disj) in the last round
r^{\star}
.
The last round
r^{\star}<\infty
corresponds to the final output, by setting
{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}}:={\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r^{\star})}};B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}:=B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r^{\star})}
and defining
B_{z_{i}}\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{\star}}}\ B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
if and only if
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r^{\star}}}}\ j
.

The cover-expansion algorithm.

(input)

(B_{z_{i}})_{i\in[m]}
and
{\mathcal{L}}_{i}={\mathcal{L}}\cap B_{z_{i}}
satisfying (
4.5
) with
\nu={\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}
and
\delta=1/2
.

(init.)

Set
r:=0
, and allocate
j\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{0}}}\ j
for all
j\leq m
.

(while)

If
(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}}
in (
4.15
) are all pairwise disjoint, set
r^{\star}:=r
; and return
{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}}:={\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r^{\star})}};B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}:=B_{j}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r^{\star})}
and
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{\star}}}:={\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r^{\star}}}}
.

Otherwise, let
j_{1}(r)\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
be the label corresponding to the largest box
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
with an overlap with some other box in round
r
, and let
j_{2}(r)
be the label of the largest box that overlaps with
B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
(using an arbitrary tie-breaking rule).
Define

(4.16)

\hskip 20.0pt\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}:=\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{(r)}\cap\big\{i:\|z_{i}-z_{j_{1}(r)}\|\leq\sqrt{d}\mathrm{Vol}(B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)})^{1/d}\big\}_{i\leq m};\qquad\mathcal{I}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}:=\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{(r)}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}.

Then we define
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}
by only re-allocating labels in
\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{(r)}
as follows:

(i)

for
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
we allocate
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}\ j_{1}(r)
, i.e., the labels of cells that are sufficiently close to the center of
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
in order to satisfy (
4.9
) are re-allocated to
j_{1}(r)
;

(ii)

for
i\in\mathcal{I}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
we allocate
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}\ i
, i.e., the labels of cells in
\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{(r)}
that are potentially too far away from the center of
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}
are re-allocated back to themselves;

(iii)

for
i\in[m]\setminus(\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})
, we set
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}\ k
if and only if
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ k
(that is,
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}
agrees with
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}
outside labels in
\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
).

Increase
r
by one and repeat (while).

We make the immediate observation:

Observation 4.10
.

In each iteration of (while),
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
in (
4.16
) is always non-empty. Moreover,

(4.17)

\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}\big)-\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\big)\geq 1.

Proof.

It can be shown inductively that
j\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}\ j
holds for all
j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
. Since the boxes
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
and
B_{j_{2}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
overlap, the distance of their centers
\|z_{j_{2}(r)}-z_{j_{1}(r)}\|
is at most the diameter of
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
, which is
\sqrt{d}\mathrm{Vol}(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)})^{1/d}
. Hence,
j_{2}(r)\in{\mathcal{I}}_{1}^{(r)}
.
Since each cell contains
\ell_{i}\geq{\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}
many vertices by the assumption in (input), we obtain by (
4.15
)

\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}\big)-\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\big)\geq\frac{\ell_{j_{2}(r)}}{{\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}}\geq 1.\qed

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

Once having shown that a cover expansion of
{\mathcal{L}}
exists, the bound on its volume (
4.11
) holds by Observation
4.9
(v). So it remains to show that the algorithm produces in finitely many rounds an output satisfying all conditions of a cover expansion in Definition
4.7
.

The algorithm stops in finitely many rounds.

We argue using a monotonicity argument. We say that a vector
\bm{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{m})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
is non-increasing if
a_{i}\geq a_{i+1}
for all
i\leq m-1
. We use the lexicographic ordering for non-increasing vectors
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
: let
\bm{a}>_{L}\bm{b}
if there exists a coordinate
j\leq m
such that
a_{\ell}=b_{\ell}
for all
\ell<j
and
a_{\ell}>b_{\ell}
for
\ell=j
.

For all
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}\subseteq[m]
, and hence,
m^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}:=|{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}|\leq m
. Let
\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{m}
be the non-increasing vector of the re-ordered
(\mathrm{Vol}(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}))_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}}
appended with
(m-m^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})
-many zeroes.
By Observation
4.10
,
the entry corresponding to
\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{1}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\big)
in
\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
increases in
\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
. Moreover, the entry corresponding to
\mathrm{Vol}\big(B_{{j_{2}(r)}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\big)
“crumbles” into smaller volumes (corresponding to boxes with label in
{\mathcal{I}}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})
that appear in
\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
. Since by definition,
j_{1}(r)
corresponds to the largest box among
(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}}
that has an overlap with some other box, so also
\mathrm{Vol}(B_{j_{2}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})\leq\mathrm{Vol}(B_{j_{1}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})
, and the allocation of labels except those in
\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
remains unchanged, these together imply that
\bm{a}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}>_{L}\bm{a}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
.
Finally, for any
r
and any
j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
,
\mathrm{Vol}(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})\leq|{\mathcal{L}}|/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d})=:b
by (
4.8
), implying that for all
r
,
(b,\dots,b)>_{L}\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
.
So,
(\bm{a}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)})_{r\geq 0}
is an increasing bounded sequence with respect to
>_{L}
, with an increase of at least
1
per step by (
4.17
). Hence,
(\bm{a}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}})_{r\geq 0}
converges and attains its limit after finitely many rounds, i.e.,
r^{\star}<\infty
.

The output corresponds to a cover expansion.

We now prove that the output
{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}},\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{\star}}}
and the corresponding boxes in (
4.15
) satisfy the conditions of Definition
4.7
. By the stopping condition in step (while) of the algorithm,
(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}}}
satisfy (disj.), and by their definition in (
4.15
), also (vol.). We need to still verify (near). We show this by induction:
initially, for
(B_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}})_{j\in{\mathcal{J}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}}},{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{0}}}
, (near) holds, since in (init.) all labels are allocated to themselves, so
\mathrm{Cells}_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}}=\{j\}
, and thus the left-hand side in (
4.9
) is
0
.
Assume then
r>0
. We prove that (near) holds for
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}
, assuming that it holds for
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r}}}
. Recall from (while) that
j_{1}(r)
is the label of the largest box that has an overlap;
j_{2}(r)
is the label of the largest box overlapping with
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
; by (
4.16
),
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
is the set of labels in
\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
re-allocated to
j_{1}(r)
, and
\mathcal{I}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}=\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
is the set labels allocated in round
r
to
j_{2}(r)
, and in round
r+1
to themselves. We distinguish between four cases for the proof of the inductive step:

•

Assume
i\notin(\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(r)}}\cup\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(r)}})
and let
k
be such that
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}k
. By (while) part (iii),
\mathrm{Cells}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}=\mathrm{Cells}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
, so by the induction hypothesis, (
4.9
) holds for
{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}
.

•

Assume
i\in\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
. Since
B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\subsetneq B_{j_{1}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r+1)}
by (
4.15
) and (
4.17
), considering
\|z_{i}-z_{j_{1}(r)}\|
and the left-hand side in (
4.9
) stay the same, while the right-hand side increases, so the inequality required for (near) still holds.

•

Assume
i\in\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}\subseteq\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}(r)}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}
. The definition of
\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
in (
4.16
) forces that
\|z_{i}-z_{j_{1}(r)}\|
satisfies (
4.9
).

•

Assume
i\in\mathcal{I}_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}=\mathrm{Cells}_{j_{2}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}\setminus\mathcal{I}_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(r)}}
: (
4.9
) holds for the same reason as for the base case, i.e., since
i\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt\mapsto}\limits^{r+1}}}\ i
,
\|z_{i}-z_{i}\|=0
trivially satisfies (
4.9
).

Having all possible cases covered, this finishes the proof of the induction. Since
r^{\star}<\infty
, this finishes the proof of Proposition
4.8
.
∎

4.3.
Poisson point processes are expandable

We end this section by showing that a Poisson point process is typically
s
-expandable for
s
sufficiently large. Recall
\Lambda_{n}=[-n^{1/d}/2,n^{1/d}/2]^{d}
.

Lemma 4.11

(PPPs are expandable)
.

Let
\Gamma
be a Poisson point process on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
equipped with an absolutely continuous intensity measure
\mu
such that
\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\leq\mathrm{Leb}(\mathrm{d}x)
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\ref{lem:expandable-ppp}}>0
such that for any
s>0
,

\mathbb{P}\big(\Gamma\cap\Lambda_{n}\text{ is not }s\text{-expandable}\big)\leq C_{\ref{lem:expandable-ppp}}n\exp(-s).

Proof.

Using stochastic domination of point processes, without loss of generality we can assume that
\Gamma
has intensity measure
\mathrm{Leb}(\mathrm{d}x)
. Let us define

\mathrm{R}(s):=\{s+\ell/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}):\ell\in\mathbb{N}\}
,
the range of volumes of boxes that we need to consider in Definition
4.1
.
By a union bound over the at most
n
possible centers of the boxes in
\Lambda_{n}
, and by translation invariance of
\mathrm{Leb}
, it holds that

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\Gamma\cap\Lambda_{n}\text{ is not }s\text{-expandable}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\big(\exists x\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\cap\Lambda_{n},\exists s^{\prime}\in\mathrm{R}(s):|\Lambda_{s^{\prime}}(x)\cap\Gamma|\geq{\mathrm{e}}s^{\prime}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\mathrm{R}(s)}\mathbb{P}\big(|\Lambda_{s^{\prime}}\cap\Gamma|\geq{\mathrm{e}}s^{\prime}\big).

Since the intensity of
\Gamma
is equal to one, by Lemma
C.1
, each summand is at most
\exp(-s^{\prime})
on the right-hand side.
Hence, using that
\sum_{\ell=a}^{\infty}f(\ell)\leq\int_{a-1}^{\infty}f(x)\mathrm{d}x
for a monotone non-increasing function, we obtain for the summation in (
4.18
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\big(\Gamma\cap\Lambda_{n}\text{ is not }s\text{-expandable}\big)\leq n\exp(-s)\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\exp\big(-\ell/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d})\big)

\displaystyle\leq n\exp(-s)\int_{-1}^{\infty}\exp\big(-x/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d})\big)\mathrm{d}x=n\exp(-s){\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}\exp\big(1/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d})\big).

The proof of the lemma follows by setting
C_{\ref{lem:expandable-ppp}}={\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d}\exp\big(1/({\mathrm{e}}d^{d/2}2^{3d})\big)
.
∎

5.
Upper bound: second-largest component

The main goal of this section is to prove the following proposition for general values of
n
and
k
, which readily implies Theorem
2.4
(ii), i.e., (
2.10
).
Recall
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}=1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\tau-1)
from (
1.12
).
We restrict ourselves to
\sigma\in(\tau-2,\tau-1]
, since
\sigma\leq\tau-2
implies that
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\leq 0
. We recall from Section
3.3
that the superscript
\star
below indicates that the result does not generalize to i-KSRGs on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.

Proposition
⋆
5.1
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model in Definition
2.1
, and parameters
\alpha>1,\tau>2
and
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Assume further that also
\sigma\in(\tau-2,\tau-1]
. There exists a constant
c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}}>0
such that whenever
n>k^{1+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}

it holds that

(5.1)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{n}|\geq k\big)\leq(n/c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}})\exp\big(-c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big).

We follow the steps of the methodology from Section
3.1.1
. The bulk of the work is to establish Steps 1 and 3, since we already developed the cover expansion of Step 4 in Section
4
.
We first introduce some notation.
We aim to partition the box
\Lambda_{n}
into disjoint subboxes of (roughly) volume
k
. Define

(5.2)

n^{\prime}:=k\lfloor(n/k)^{1/d}\rfloor^{d}.

The box
\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}\subseteq\Lambda_{n}
is the largest box inside
\Lambda_{n}
that can be partitioned into
n^{\prime}/k
disjoint subboxes of volume exactly
k
(boundaries are allocated uniquely similar to Definition
4.3
). Let the boxes of this partitioning of
\Lambda_{n^{\prime}}
be
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n^{\prime}/k}
, labeled so that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
shares a boundary (that is, a
(d-1)
-dimensional face) with
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i+1}
for all
i<n^{\prime}/k
.
Define for each
u=(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi_{n}\subset\Lambda_{n}

(5.3)

{\mathcal{Q}}(u):=\argmin_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}\|x_{u}-{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\|,

with the convention that
\|x_{u}-{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\|=0
if
x_{u}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
, and take the box with the smallest index if the minimum is non-unique.
Similarly to (
4.4
), we observe that for any point
u\in\Xi_{n}\subset\Lambda_{n}

(5.4)

\sup_{y\in{\mathcal{Q}}(u)}\|x_{u}-y\|\leq 2\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}.

Step 1. Construction of the backbone

Recall the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[a,b)
from (
2.4
) in Definition
2.1
.
We first show that, for some
w_{\mathrm{hh}}=w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k)
, the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}:={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
contains a so-called backbone, a connected component
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
that contains at least
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
vertices in every subbox.
Using
\alpha
and
\beta
from Definition
2.1
, define the constant
C_{1}
to be the solution of the equation

(5.5)

\displaystyle(p/16)\beta^{\alpha}C_{1}^{1-(\sigma+1)\alpha/(\tau-1)}(2\sqrt{d})^{-\alpha d}

\displaystyle=\log(2),

\displaystyle\mbox{if }\alpha

\displaystyle<\infty,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\beta C_{1}^{-(1+\sigma)/(\tau-1)}d^{-d/2}2^{-d-2\sigma}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\mbox{if }\alpha

\displaystyle=\infty.

We set, with
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}
from (
1.11
),

(5.7)

w_{\mathrm{hh}}:=w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k):=C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}},\qquad s_{k}:=(C_{1}/16)k^{1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\tau-1)}=kw_{\mathrm{hh}}^{-(\tau-1)}/16.

To avoid cumbersome notation, we assume that
s_{k}\in\mathbb{N}
.
Recall the notation
\Xi_{\mathcal{Q}}[a,b)
from (
2.12
). Let

(5.8)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}:={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}\text{ contains a connected component }{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)\text{ such that }\\
&\text{ for all }i\leq(n^{\prime}/k):|\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}|\geq s_{k}\end{aligned}\right\}.

On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, let
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}:={\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
, the backbone, be the largest component in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
that satisfies the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
.
In the following lemma we obtain a lower bound on the probability that there exists a backbone. Observe that
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
in (
5.7
), so the decay on the right-hand side in (
5.9
) below is of the same order as the desired decay in Proposition
5.1
.

Lemma 5.2

(Backbone construction)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
, and parameters
\alpha>1
,
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
,
\sigma\geq 0
,
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
There exist constants
c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}=c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}(p,\beta,d,\alpha,\tau,\sigma)>0
and
k_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that for
k\geq k_{1}
and all
n
satisfying

n\geq k^{1+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}

we have

(5.9)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)\big)\leq 3(n/k)\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}s_{k}\big).

Proof.

Towards proving (
5.9
), we reveal
\Xi_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, i.e.,
only
the vertex set of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
, and define

(5.10)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}:=\{\forall i\leq n^{\prime}/k:|\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})|\geq 4s_{k}\}.

On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}
, every box contains enough vertices in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
.
Reveal now the edges of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}

only within each of the boxes

({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})_{i\leq n^{\prime}/k}
: let
{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}
be the induced subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
on
\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, and define

(5.11)

J:=\min\left\{i:{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\mbox{\ contains a connected component\ }{\mathcal{C}}\mbox{\ with\ }|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq s_{k}\right\}.

We write
J=\infty
if no such box-index exists. Then

(5.12)

\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}})\leq\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})+\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})+\sum_{i=1}^{n^{\prime}/k}\mathbb{P}(J=i\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid\{J=i\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}).

We first bound
\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})
from above.
The distribution of
|\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})|
is Poisson
with mean
kw_{\mathrm{hh}}^{-(\tau-1)}(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})=16(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})s_{k}\geq 8s_{k}
by (
2.2
), (
5.7
) and since
\tau\geq 2
. Lemma
C.1
yields

\mathbb{P}\big(|\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})|\leq 4s_{k}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(8s_{k})<4s_{k}\big)\leq\exp\big(-4s_{k}(1-\log(2))\big).

Since
1-\log(2)\geq 1/4
, by a union bound over the at most
n^{\prime}/k\leq n/k
subboxes we get

(5.13)

\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})\leq(n/k)\exp(-s_{k}).

We will now show an upper bound on the summands in (
5.12
) that holds uniformly in
i
. For this, we iteratively ‘construct’ a backbone. The subboxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\dots,{\mathcal{Q}}_{n^{\prime}/k}
are ordered so that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i+1}
share a boundary for all
i
. On
\{J=i\}
, we know that
{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}
inside
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
contains a connected component
{\mathcal{C}}
with at least
s_{k}
many vertices. We now reveal edges between
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i+1}
, and bound the probability that there are at least
s_{k}
many vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i+1}
that are connected by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}
: denote this set of vertices by
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i+1}
. Next, we apply the same bound to show that at least
s_{k}
many vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i+2}
connect by an edge to
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i+1}
, and so on.
For
\ell<i
, we proceed similarly. Although
\{J=i\}
implies that the
induced
graph
{\mathcal{H}}_{\ell,1}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell}
does not contain a large enough connected component, we can, thanks to conditioning on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}
, still ensure that at least
s_{k}
many vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}
connect directly by an edge to the connected component
{\mathcal{C}}
in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
,
irrespective of the vertex positions in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i-1}
. Again, denote these vertices by
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i-1}
. We repeat this procedure for
\ell\in\{i-2,\dots,1\}
.
Hence, for
\ell\geq i
, we need to analyze the probability that a vertex in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell+1}
connects to a vertex in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}
, conditionally on
|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq s_{k}
.
Since by assumption
\tau<2+\sigma
, by definition of
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}
in (
1.11
) for all
\tau<2+\sigma
and
\alpha\leq\infty
, it holds that

(5.14)

1-(1+\sigma)\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}\geq 0,\quad\mbox{and}\quad 2+\sigma-\tau>0.

Let
c_{d}
be the volume of the unit
d
-dimensional ball. Then let
k_{1}
be the smallest integer so that, depending on the value of
\alpha
, the following bounds hold (with
C_{1}=C_{1}(\alpha)
from (
5.5
)-(
5.6
), respectively):

(5.15)

\displaystyle(1-p)^{C_{1}k_{1}^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}/16}

\displaystyle\leq 1/2,\qquad\mbox{and }\qquad(C_{1}/4)k_{1}^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(p\beta c_{d}2^{-d}\big(C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k_{1}\big)^{(2+\sigma-\tau)\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}/8\big).

The Euclidean distance between vertices in neighboring boxes is at most
2\sqrt{d}k^{1/d}
(twice the diameter of a single box), and all considered vertices have mark at least
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
.
When
\alpha=\infty
, we use that
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}=1/(1+\sigma)
(see (
1.11
)), and so
w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{1+\sigma}/k=\Theta(1)
by (
5.7
). We obtain using (
5.6
),
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
), that for any
u=(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell+1}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
,

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((x_{u},w_{u})\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}\mid|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq s_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\Big(1-p\mathbbm{1}\Big\{\frac{\beta w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{1+\sigma}}{(2\sqrt{d})^{d}k}\geq 1\Big\}\Big)^{s_{k}}=1-(1-p)^{s_{k}}\geq 1/2,

for all
k\geq k_{1}
by the first criterion in (
5.15
).
When
\alpha<\infty
, using
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
) for
u\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell+1}
with
w_{u}\geq w_{\mathrm{hh}}
, either the minimum is at
1
below in (
5.17
) (in which case the right-hand side of (
5.16
) remains valid) or, the minimum in
\mathrm{p}
is attained at the second term below in (
5.17
): then we substitute
s_{k}
from (
5.7
),

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big((x_{u},w_{u})\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}\mid|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq s_{k}\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\big(1-p\min\big\{1,\beta^{\alpha}(2\sqrt{d})^{-\alpha d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}k^{-\alpha}\big\}\big)^{s_{k}}

\displaystyle=1-\big(1-p\beta^{\alpha}(2\sqrt{d})^{-\alpha d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}k^{-\alpha}\big)^{kw_{\mathrm{hh}}^{-(\tau-1)}/16}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\big(-(p/16)\beta^{\alpha}(2\sqrt{d})^{-\alpha d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha-(\tau-1)}k^{1-\alpha}\big).

By choice of
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}
and
C_{1}
and as defined in (
5.7
), (
5.5
), and (
1.11
), respectively, factors containing
k
cancel, and after simplification we arrive at

(5.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(u\leftrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle\mid|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq s_{k}\big)\geq 1-\exp\big(-(p/16)\beta^{\alpha}(2\sqrt{d})^{-\alpha d}C_{1}^{1-(1+\sigma)\alpha/(\tau-1)}\big)=1/2.

Combining (
5.18
) with (
5.16
), we obtain a lower bound of
1/2
for all
\alpha>1
for any
u\in\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell+1}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
.
On
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}
(see (
5.10
)) there are at least
4s_{k}
vertices in
\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\ell+1}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
. Each of these vertices connects conditionally independently by an edge to vertices in
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}
with probability at least
1/2
, so for all
\ell\geq i

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell+1}|\geq s_{k}\mid|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq 4s_{k},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}\big(\,{\mathrm{Bin}}(4s_{k},1/2\,)\geq s_{k}\big)\geq 1-\exp(-s_{k}/4),

where the last bound follows by Chernoff’s bound, see e.g.
[
40
, Theorem 2.1]
. When
J=i
and
\ell<i
, we can analogously bound the probability that
|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}|\geq s_{k}
, conditionally on
|\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell+1}|\geq s_{k}
.
By a union bound over the at most
n^{\prime}/k
subboxes (with indices both smaller as well as larger than
i
), we obtain

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid\{J=i\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}\big)\leq(n^{\prime}/k)\exp(-s_{k}/4)\leq(n/k)\exp(-s_{k}/4).

The bound holds for all
i\leq n^{\prime}/k
. Using this in the summation on the right-hand side of (
5.12
), and using (
5.13
) to bound
\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})
, (
5.12
) turns into

(5.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(n/k)\exp(-s_{k})+\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})+(n/k)\exp(-s_{k}/4)\mathbb{P}(J\neq\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})

\displaystyle\leq 2(n/k)\exp(-s_{k}/4)+\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}).

It remains to bound
\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})
, with
J
from (
5.11
). For this we show that the graph
{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}
induced on
\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
stochastically dominates a soft random geometric graph above its connectivity threshold.
Indeed, consider
(x_{u},w_{u})
and
(x_{v},w_{v})
in
\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
.
Then using (
2.3
), for
\alpha=\infty
,

\mathrm{p}\big((x_{u},w_{u}),(x_{v},w_{v}))\geq p\min\big\{1,\beta^{\alpha}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{-d\alpha}\big\}\geq p,

whenever
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\leq\beta^{1/d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)/d}=:r_{\mathrm{hh}}
. The calculation for
\alpha=\infty
is analogous yielding the same radius
r_{\mathrm{hh}}
and same lower bound
p
. Hence,
{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{RG_{i}}
where in
\mathrm{RG}_{i}
is a soft random geometric graph: conditioned on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}}
, there are say
N_{i}\geq 4s_{k}
many vertices in
\Xi_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, and two vertices are connected by an edge with probability
p
whenever they are within distance
r_{\mathrm{hh}}=\beta^{1/d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)/d}
of each other.
Writing
B_{r}
for the Euclidean ball of radius
r
around the origin and for some dimension dependent constant
c_{d}
, the expected degree of a vertex at
any
location in
\mathrm{RG}_{i}
is thus, conditional on
N_{i}
:

(5.20)

\mathbb{E}[\deg_{\mathrm{RG}_{i}}(v)\mid N_{i}]\geq pN_{i}\frac{\mathrm{Vol}(B_{r_{\mathrm{hh}}})/2^{d}}{k}=N_{i}\cdot pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d}\cdot w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{1+\sigma}/k.

We apply
[
61
, Equation (2.6) below Theorem 2.1]
to
\mathrm{RG_{i}}
, and combine it with
{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\succcurlyeq\mathrm{RG}_{i}
to obtain

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\mbox{ not connected}\mid N_{i})\leq\mathbb{P}(\mathrm{RG_{i}}\mbox{ not connected}\mid N_{i})\leq N_{i}\exp\big(-\mathbb{E}[\deg_{\mathrm{RG}_{i}}(v)\mid N_{i}]\,\big).

On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}},N_{i}\geq 4s_{k}
holds for all
i
. Hence, by (
5.20
), uniformly for all
i\leq n^{\prime}/k
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\mbox{ not connected}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{N_{i}\geq 4s_{k}}N_{i}\exp\big(-\mathbb{E}[\deg_{\mathrm{RG}_{i}}(v)\mid N_{i}]\,\big)

\displaystyle\leq 4s_{k}\exp\big(-4s_{k}\cdot pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d}\cdot w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{1+\sigma}/k\big)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{1}/4)k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\exp\big(-pc_{d}\beta w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{2+\sigma-\tau}/4\big),

where we used
s_{k}
from (
5.7
) to obtain the last row.
Using the second criterion in (
5.15
), for all
k\geq k_{1}
,

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\mbox{ not connected}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})\leq\exp(-pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{2+\sigma-\tau}/8)=:p_{1}.

By (
5.14
), the exponent of
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
is positive.
Since the induced subgraphs
({\mathcal{H}}_{i,1})_{i\leq n^{\prime}/k}
are independent (being contained in disjoint boxes
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
), and the number of boxes is
n^{\prime}/k=\lfloor(n/k)^{1/d}\rfloor^{d}\geq n/(2k)
, using the definition of
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
from (
5.7
), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(\forall i\leq n^{\prime}/k:{\mathcal{H}}_{i,1}\mbox{ not connected}\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})\leq p_{1}^{n/(2k)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d-4}(n/k)w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{2+\sigma-\tau}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-(pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d-4})C_{1}^{-(2+\sigma-\tau)(\tau-1)}(n/k)k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(2+\sigma-\tau)}\big).

Using (
1.11
) and (
1.12
), it is elementary to check that
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(2+\sigma-\tau)=\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}(1-1/\alpha)
. Hence, whenever
n/k>k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}
, the exponent of
k
on the right-hand side is (at least)
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
.
Since we assumed
n\geq k^{1+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}
, this is indeed the case. Furthermore, since
s_{k}=(C_{1}/16)k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
by (
5.7
), with
c_{2}:=(pc_{d}\beta 2^{-d})C_{1}^{-(2+\sigma-\tau)(\tau-1)-1}
, using
C_{1}
from (
5.5
)), we obtain that

\mathbb{P}(J=\infty\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{poi}})\leq\exp(-c_{2}s_{k}).

Combined with (
5.19
), this yields the statement of the lemma in (
5.9
) with
c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}:=\min\{c_{2},1/4\}
.
∎

We will end Step 1 with a claim that shows (
3.2
), using notation for the construction of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
that facilitates later steps that we introduce first.
We recall the definition of i-KSRG from Definition
2.1
.
Given the vertex set
\Xi
, it is standard practice to use independent uniform random variables to facilitate couplings with the edge-set. This definition here is more general and allows for other helping random variables as well, leading to different distributions on graphs. This will be useful later.

Definition 5.3

(Graph encoding)
.

Let
\xi\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[1,\infty)
be a discrete set and assume that

\Psi_{\xi}=\big\{\varphi_{u,v}:\varphi_{u,v}\in[0,1],\{u,v\}\in\binom{\xi}{2}\big\}
is a collection of random variables given
\xi
.
For a given connectivity function
\mathrm{p}:({\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[1,\infty))^{2}\to[0,1]
,
we call
{\mathcal{G}}^{\prime}=({\mathcal{V}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
the (sub)graph
encoded by

(\xi,\Psi_{\xi},\mathrm{p})
if
{\mathcal{V}}^{\prime}=\xi
and for all
\{u,v\}\in\binom{\xi}{2}
, with
u=(x_{u},w_{u}),v=(x_{v},w_{v})
,

(5.21)

\big\{\{u,v\}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\big\}\Longleftrightarrow\big\{\varphi_{u,v}\leq\mathrm{p}\big((x_{u},w_{u}),(x_{v},w_{v})\big)\big\}.

Given
\Xi
in (
2.2
),
and
\mathrm{p}
from (
2.3
), let
\Psi_{\Xi}
be a collection of independent
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables given
\Xi
.
{\mathcal{G}}_{\infty}
in Definition
2.1
is then the graph
encoded by

(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi},\mathrm{p})
.
Writing
\Psi_{n}[a,b):=\big\{\varphi_{u,v}\in\Psi_{\Xi}:\{u,v\}\in\binom{\Xi_{n}[a,b)}{2}\}
and
\Psi_{n}:=\Psi_{n}[1,\infty)
,
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
in (
2.4
) is then the graph encoded by
(\Xi_{n},\Psi_{n},\mathrm{p})
.

An immediate corollary is the following:

Corollary 5.4
.

Assume
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}},\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
are two random graphs, encoded respectively by
(\widetilde{\Xi},\widetilde{\Psi},\mathrm{p})
, and
(\widehat{\Xi},\widehat{\Psi},\mathrm{p})
for respective point processes
\widetilde{\Xi},\widehat{\Xi}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\times[1,\infty)
using the
same
connectivity function
\mathrm{p}
. If
(\widetilde{\Xi},\widetilde{\Psi})
and
(\widehat{\Xi},\widehat{\Psi})
have the same law then the encoded graphs

\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
also
have the same law.

The collection of (conditionally) independent
uniform
variables
\Psi_{n}=\{\varphi_{u,v}:\{u,v\}\in\binom{\Xi_{n}}{2}\}
and the connectivity function
\mathrm{p}
determine the presence of edges in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
. By (
5.21
), if for some
r>0
it holds that
\varphi_{u,v}\leq r\leq\mathrm{p}(u,v)
, then
\{u\leftrightarrow v\}
.
Writing
{\mathcal{Q}}(u)
for the box containing or closest to
u\in\Xi_{n}
(see (
5.3
)), let
v_{u}(1),v_{u}(2),\dots,v_{u}(s_{k}),\dots
denote the vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}(u)\cap{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, in decreasing order with respect to their marks. Let

(5.22)

{\mathcal{S}}(u):=\big\{v_{u}(1),\dots,v_{u}(s_{k})\big\}.

Claim 5.5

(Connections to the backbone)
.

Consider an i-KSRG satisfying the conditions of Proposition
5.1
.
Fix
n
and
k
and assume
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
satisfies the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
.
Let
\Psi_{n}=\{\varphi_{u,v}:\{u,v\}\in\binom{\Xi_{n}}{2}\}
be a collection of iid
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables and
r_{k}:=1-2^{-1/s_{k}}
. Then, for all
u\in\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k),\infty)
and
v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)
,
\mathrm{p}(u,v)\geq r_{k}
and

(5.23)

\mathbb{P}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}>r_{k}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)=\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}\leq r_{k}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)=1/2.

Proof.

On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
satisfies (
5.8
) and in particular
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
in (
5.22
) is well-defined and has size
s_{k}
. Since
\{\varphi_{u,v}\}
is a collection of iid
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables, (cf. Definition
5.3
), one
must
set
r_{k}:=1-2^{-1/s_{k}}
for (
5.23
) to hold. Hence, it only remains to show
\mathrm{p}(u,v)\geq r_{k}
in the statement.

With
{\mathcal{Q}}(u)
and
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
from (
5.3
) and (
5.22
), respectively, by (
5.4
), every
u\in\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
is at distance at most
2\sqrt{d}k
from any vertex in
v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)
.
Since
w_{u}\geq 2w_{\mathrm{hh}}\geq w_{\mathrm{hh}}
, and
|{\mathcal{S}}(u)|=s_{k}
, the computations (
5.16
) – (
5.18
) word-by-word carry through with
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\ell}
replaced by
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
, obtaining

\mathbb{P}(u\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}(u)\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1},\Xi_{n},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}})=1-\prod_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v))\geq 1-(1-z_{k})^{s_{k}}\geq 1/2,

with
z_{k}
either equaling
p
in the right-hand side of (
5.16
) or the appropriate expression in the right-hand side of (
5.17
), that bound
individually
each
\mathrm{p}(u,v)
from below. Following now the calculations towards (
5.18
) ensures that in both cases

z_{k}\geq 1-2^{-1/s_{k}}
. The assumptions on
k\geq k_{1}
in (
5.15
) are also needed for this (for instance that
p\geq r_{k}
).
∎

Step 2. Revealing low-mark vertices

Having established that
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
contains a backbone with sufficiently high probability, we define
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}:={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\supseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
.

Step 3. Presampling the vertices connecting to the backbone

We make Step 3 of Section
3.1.1
precise now. Step 3 ensures that during Step 4 below no
small-to-large
merging occurs when revealing the connector vertices of
\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
. That is, components of size smaller than
k
do not merge into a larger component via edges to a vertex
v\in\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
that is not connected to the backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
(
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
will be contained in the giant component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
). So, we partially pre-sample some randomness that encodes the presence of some edges.

For a pair
n,k
, we now present the alternative graph-encoding
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
of KSRGs (cf. Definitions
2.1
and
5.3
) and verify that
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
in Definition
2.1
have the same law. The difference between the encoding in Definition
5.3
and the construction of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is that in the latter the edge-variables
\varphi_{u,v}
are no longer independent
{\mathrm{Unif}}[0,1]
random variables, but are sampled from a suitable (conditional) joint distribution, whenever
u\in\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k),\infty)
and
v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)
from (
5.22
). Recall
r_{k}=1-2^{-1/s_{k}}
from Claim
5.5
, with
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k):=w_{\mathrm{hh}}
and
s_{k}
defined in (
5.7
).

Definition 5.6

(Alternative graph construction)
.

Fix
n
and
k
.
Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k))
from Definition
2.1
be the graph
encoded by
\big(\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}),\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}),\mathrm{p}\big)
.
Let

\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
and
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)

be two
independent
Poisson point processes on
\Lambda_{n}\times[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
with intensity
(1/2)\mathrm{Leb}\otimes F_{W}(\mathrm{d}w)
, with
F_{W}
as in (
2.2
), and let

(5.24)

\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty):=\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty).

Let
\Sigma_{n}:=\{U_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\}
be a collection of iid
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables (conditionally on these PPPs).

(i) If
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
does not satisfy the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
5.8
), then set
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}:=\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\Sigma_{n}
in Definition
5.3
to construct
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\supseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
on
\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, i.e.,

\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}:=(\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\Sigma_{n},\mathrm{p}).

(ii) If
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
satisfies the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, then we construct
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\supseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}

conditionally on

{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
as follows.
For each
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
in (
5.24
), the set of vertices
{\mathcal{S}}(u)\subseteq\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
is a deterministic function of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
, given by (
5.22
).
Let

(5.25)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{unsure})}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{U_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(unsure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(unsure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\cup\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\setminus{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{sure})}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{U_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(sure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(sure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\cup\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\setminus{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\},

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{both})}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{U_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(sure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{(unsure)}}}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big\}

be disjoint subsets of
\Sigma_{n}
, and write
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid})}}:=\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{unsure})}}\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{sure})}}\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{both})}}
for the union.
Conditionally on
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, define also the collections of random variables

(5.26)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{cond,unsure})}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\},

(5.27)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{cond,sure})}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}:u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\},

so that for different vertices
u_{1},u_{2}\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, the collections
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u_{1},v}\}_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u_{1})}
and
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u_{2},v^{\prime}}\}_{v^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}(u_{2})}
are
independent
. The joint distribution of
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\}_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
for a single
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
is as follows: for
any
sequence
(z_{u,v})_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}\in[0,1]^{{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
of length
s_{k}
, and with
r_{k}=1-2^{-1/s_{k}}
,

(5.28)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}

\displaystyle\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}>r_{k}\big).

Similarly we define the joint distribution of
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\}_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
for a single
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
as follows: for any sequence
(z_{u,v})_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}\in[0,1]^{{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
of length
s_{k}
,

(5.29)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}

\displaystyle\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big)

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\exists v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq r_{k}\big).

We define
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
as the graph encoded by
(\widehat{\Xi}_{n},\widehat{\Psi}_{n},\mathrm{p})
, where

(5.30)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Xi}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\cup\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi}_{n}

\displaystyle:=\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid})}}\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{cond,unsure})}}\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{cond,sure})}}.

An immediate corollary is the following statement.

Corollary 5.7
.

Consider an i-KSRG
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
following Definition
5.6
for some
n,k
.
On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
, every vertex in
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
is connected by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
in
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
.

Proof.

The conditioning in (
5.29
) guarantees that for each
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
at least one
\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq r_{k}
occurs among the edge-variables
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}:v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)\}
, where
{\mathcal{S}}(u)\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
(cf. (
5.22
)).
Then since
\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq r_{k}\leq\mathrm{p}(u,v)
holds by Claim
5.5
, this ensures that
\{u,v\}
is in the edge set of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
by the graph-encoding in Definition
5.3
.
∎

Proposition 5.8
.

Fix a connectivity function
\mathrm{p}
. The law of the random graph
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
formed by Definition
5.6
is identical to the law of the random graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
formed by Definition
5.3
.

Proof.

By Corollary
5.4
it is sufficient to show that
(\widehat{\Xi}_{n},\widehat{\Psi}_{n})
defined in (
5.30
) has the same distribution as
(\Xi_{n},\Psi_{n})
from Definition
5.6
. By standard properties of PPPs,
\Xi_{n}
can be written as two independent PPPs defined on the same space, each having half the intensity measure of
\Xi_{n}
. Thus, by the construction of
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
in Definition
5.6
, it holds that the PPPs
\Xi_{n}
and
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}
have the same law. Hence, we may couple the vertex sets so that a.s.
\Xi_{n}=\widehat{\Xi}_{n}
, and only need to show that the collection of variables encoding the edges,
\Psi_{n}
and
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}
, share the same law, conditionally under the given vertex set realization (say)
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}
.
By (
5.30
) in Definition
5.6
, the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
spanned on
\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\subseteq\widehat{\Xi}_{n}
is determined by
\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})=\{\varphi_{u,v}:u,v\in\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\}
in Definition
5.3
. Thus
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
has the same distribution both in Definition
5.3
and in Definition
5.6
.

(i) If now
\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
is such that the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
does
not
satisfy the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, by (i) of Definition
5.6
, the statement holds since both
\{\varphi_{u,v}\}
and
\{U_{u,v}\}
are iid uniforms whenever
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, i.e.,
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}\setminus\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})=\Sigma_{n}
and
\Psi_{n}\setminus\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
have the same distribution.

(ii) If
\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
is such that the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
does satisfy the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, then we work conditionally on a realization of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}=(\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}),\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}),\mathrm{p})
, and also on the coupled realization of the PPPs
\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)=\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
. Let us define the conditional probability measure (of the edges) under the coupling by

(5.31)

\mathbb{P}^{\star}(\cdot):=\mathbb{P}(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty))=\mathbb{P}(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},\mbox{unlabeled }\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)),

where in the conditioning we do
not
reveal to which sub-PPP (
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
or
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
) a vertex in
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
belongs to.
Using
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}
from (
5.30
) and
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid})}\subseteq\Sigma_{n}
from (
5.25
) (containing
independent
copies
U_{u,v}
of
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables, like
\Psi
in Definition
5.3
), we see that variables in
\Psi_{n}\setminus\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
and
\widehat{\Psi}_{n}\setminus\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
also share the same (joint) law of iid
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
whenever
u
and
v
are such
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh},\infty})
and that
v\notin{\mathcal{S}}(u)
.
Moreover, in (
5.26
)-(
5.27
), the collections
\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\}_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
are independent across
u
for different vertices
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
.
So for
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\ {\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{d}}}\ {\mathcal{G}}_{n}
it is left to show that for any fixed
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)=\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, under the measure
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
,

(5.32)

\big\{\varphi_{u,v},v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\}\overset{d}{=}\big\{\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v},v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big\}.

We first analyze the distribution of the left-hand side, i.e.,
\varphi_{u,v}
being iid from Definition
5.3
.
Let
(z_{u,v})_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}\in[0,1]^{{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
be any sequence of length
s_{k}
.
By Claim
5.5
, and the law of total probability

(5.33)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\big)

\displaystyle=(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}>r_{k}\big)

\displaystyle+(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\exists v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\varphi_{u,v}\leq r_{k}\big).

We now analyze the right-hand side in (
5.32
). By the construction in (
5.24
),
\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
is the union of two iid sub-PPPs. Under
\mathbb{P}^{\star}
in (
5.31
) we did not reveal to which sub-PPP vertices belong to. Hence, for each
u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, independently of each other

\mathbb{P}^{\star}(u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty))=\mathbb{P}^{\star}(u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty))=1/2.

Thus, by the law of total probability, and using the distributions of
(\varphi_{u,v})_{v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u)}
given by (
5.28
), (
5.29
),

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\big)

\displaystyle=(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big)

\displaystyle+(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):\widehat{\varphi}_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid u\in\widehat{\Xi}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big)

\displaystyle=(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}>r_{k}\big)

\displaystyle+(1/2)\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big(\forall v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq z_{u,v}\mid\exists v\in{\mathcal{S}}(u):U_{u,v}\leq r_{k}\big).

Note that
\{U_{u,v}\}_{u,v}
and
\{\varphi_{u,v}\}_{u,v}
are both sets of independent
\mathrm{Unif}[0,1]
random variables by Definitions
5.6
and
5.3
, respectively. Hence, (
5.32
) follows by combining (
5.33
) and (
5.34
).
∎

For the remainder of this section, we assume that we construct
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
following Definition
5.6
and write

\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty):=\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\cup\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)

as the union of two independent PPPs of equal intensity, such that if
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}={\mathcal{G}}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
5.8
), every vertex in
\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
connects by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, by Corollary
5.7
.

To finish Step 3, on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, we define
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}:=({\mathcal{V}}_{n,3},\Psi_{n,3},\mathrm{p})
, with

(5.35)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}:=\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty),\quad\Psi_{n,3}:=\Psi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{iid},\mathrm{unsure})}}\cup\widehat{\Psi}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{cond},\mathrm{unsure})}},

i.e., the graph spanned on
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
.
We call the vertices in
\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)

sure-connector
vertices. If the event
A_{\mathrm{bb}}
does not hold then we say that the construction failed and we leave
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
undefined.

Step 4. Cover expansion

In this step, we ensure that all components of size at least
k
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
merge with the giant component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
via edges towards sure-connector vertices, with error probability
\mathrm{err}_{n,k}
from (
3.1
). The next lemma proves this using the cover-expansion technique of Section
4
. The notion of expandability is from Definition
4.1
, and
s_{k}
is from (
5.7
). Let for some
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}>0

(5.36)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}):={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(n,k,c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}):=\big\{{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}\mbox{ is $(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k})$-expandable}\big\}.

Lemma
⋆
5.9

(Cover-expansion and
\sigma\leq\tau-1
)
.

Consider an i-KSRG satisfying the conditions of Proposition
5.1
.
For any
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}>0

(5.37)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime})\big)\leq C_{\ref{lem:expandable-ppp}}n\exp(-c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k}).

Moreover, if
\sigma\leq\tau-1
, there exist
k_{2},c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}},c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}>0
, such that
conditionally on any realization of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime})
,
for all
k\geq k_{2}
and any connected component
{\mathcal{C}}
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
with
|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq k
,

(5.38)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}\not\leftrightarrow\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime})\big)\leq\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}s_{k}\big).

Proof.

The statement (
5.37
) follows directly for any
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}>0
from its definition in (
5.36
) and Lemma
4.11
.
In Proposition
4.2
, for a given mark
\underline{w}
, the function
s(\underline{w})
in (
4.1
) describes the necessary “expandability parameter”, such that all vertices with mark at least
\underline{w}
in
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{L}})
connect to any
s(\underline{w})
-expandable set
{\mathcal{L}}
of vertices with probability at least
p/2
. We shall take
\underline{w}:=2w_{\mathrm{hh}}
, the lowest possible mark in
\Xi_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}}
.
By Definition
4.1
of expandability, if
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
is
(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k})
-expandable, it is also
s(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
-expandable whenever

(5.39)

s(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\geq c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k}.

We compute the left-hand side using the value of
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
from (
5.7
) and the function
s(\underline{w})
from (
4.1
):

s(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})=\big(2^{d+1}\beta C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}\big)^{1/(1-1/\alpha)}k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}/(1-1/\alpha)}.

By (
5.7
) and (
1.12
), it holds that

s_{k}=\Theta(k^{1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\tau-1)})=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
. Using (
1.12
), it is elementary to verify that
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}/(1-1/\alpha)\geq\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
if and only if
\sigma\leq\tau-1
.
Thus, if
\sigma\leq\tau-1
, (
5.39
) holds for some
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}>0
, whenever
k>k_{2}
for some sufficiently large
k_{2}\geq 1
, that ensures the condition
\underline{w}>2^{d}d^{d/2}/\beta
in Proposition
4.2
.
On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime})
,
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
is thus also
s(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
-expandable (since it is
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k}
expandable). Since expandability carries through for subsets of
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
(below Definition
4.1
),
any
subset of
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
is
s(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
-expandable. Hence, Proposition
4.2
is applicable for any set
{\mathcal{L}}\subseteq{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
and
\underline{w}:=2w_{\mathrm{hh}}
, and guarantees the existence of a set
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{L}})\subseteq\Lambda_{n}
satisfying (
4.2
) and (
4.3
).

Consider an arbitrary connected component
{\mathcal{C}}
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
that satisfies
|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq k
. With
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}})
from Proposition
4.2
, we define the set of sure-connector vertices inside
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}})
connected to
{\mathcal{C}}
as

(5.40)

{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}:=\{v\in{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}})\cap\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\big):v\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}\}.

Since
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
is a Poisson process, the number of its points in
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}})
follows a Poisson distribution. Since each of these points connects by an edge
independently
to
{\mathcal{C}}
with probability at least
p/2
by (
4.3
), and an independent thinning of a PPP is another PPP;
we obtain using the intensity measure in Definition
5.6
by the volume bound (
4.2
) on
{\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}})
for
|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq k
and
c:=(p/4)2^{-(4d+1)}2^{-(\tau-1)}d^{-d/2}/{\mathrm{e}}>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathcal{C}}|=0\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}\big((p/2)\cdot(1/2)\cdot\mathrm{Vol}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}}))\cdot(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})^{-(\tau-1)}\big)=0\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-(p/4)\mathrm{Vol}({\mathcal{K}}_{n}({\mathcal{C}}))(2w_{\mathrm{hh}})^{-(\tau-1)}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\big(-c\cdot kw_{\mathrm{hh}}^{-(\tau-1)}\big)=\exp\big(-16c\cdot s_{k}\big),

where we used
s_{k}
from (
5.7
) in the last step. Since
\{|{\mathcal{H}}_{{\mathcal{C}}}|>0\}
in (
5.40
) implies
\{{\mathcal{C}}\leftrightarrow\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\}
, this finishes the proof of (
5.38
) with
c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}:=16(p/4)2^{-(4d+1)}2^{-(\tau-1)}d^{-d/2}/{\mathrm{e}}
.
∎

Combining everything: preventing too large components

Proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Assume that
k\geq\max\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
defined in Lemmas
5.2
and
5.9
, respectively, and that
n>k^{1+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}
, i.e.,
n
satisfies the bound in Lemma
5.2
. We construct
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}\supseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
following Definition
5.6
, where
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
from (
5.35
) is the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
induced on
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}=\Xi_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})\cup\Xi_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{unsure})}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
.
The events
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
5.8
) and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}(c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}):={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}
in (
5.36
) are measurable with respect to
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
. By the law of total probability (taking expectation over realizations of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
), we obtain

(5.41)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)\big]+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}\big).

The not-yet revealed vertices after Step 3 are
{\mathcal{V}}_{n}\setminus{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}=\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
, and by Corollary
5.7
each vertex in
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
connects by an edge to
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
. Thus each component
{\mathcal{C}}\nsupseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
either remains the same in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
or it merges with the component containing
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
via a vertex in
{\mathcal{V}}\setminus{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}
. Hence, conditionally on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}
,

(5.42)

\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}_{n}|\geq k\big\}\subseteq\{\exists\mbox{ a component }{\mathcal{C}}\mbox{ of }{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3}\mbox{ with }|{\mathcal{C}}|\geq k:{\mathcal{C}}\not\leftrightarrow\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{sure})}_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)\}.

By a union bound over the at most
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}|/k
components of size at least
k
, (
5.38
) of Lemma
5.9
yields

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,3},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{exp}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)\leq(|{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}|/k)\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}s_{k}\big).

Substituting this bound into (
5.41
), and using Lemmas
5.2
and
5.9
to bound the last two terms yields

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)\leq(\mathbb{E}\big[|{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}|\big]/k)\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}s_{k}\big)+(3n/k)\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}s_{k}\big)+C_{\ref{lem:expandable-ppp}}n\exp\big(-c_{\ref{lemma:hh-expandable}}^{\prime}s_{k}\big).

Since
{\mathcal{V}}_{n,3}\subseteq\Xi_{n}
by construction,
\mathbb{E}[|\Xi_{n}|]=n
by (
2.2
), and
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
by (
5.7
), this finishes the proof of
Proposition
5.1
for
k\geq\max\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
and
n>k^{1+\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}/\alpha}
. For
k<\max\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
, (
5.1
) is trivially satisfied for
c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}}>0
sufficiently small.
∎

The backbone: intermediate results

We state two corollaries of the proof of Proposition
5.1
, and two propositions based on the backbone constructions for later use. We defer the detailed proofs to the appendix and only give a sketch here. We start with a corollary of the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Corollary
⋆
5.10

(Backbone becoming part of the giant)
.

Consider an i-KSRG satisfying the conditions of Proposition
5.1
.
Then conditionally on the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
in (
5.8
),

(5.43)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)\nsubseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)\big)\leq(n/c_{\ref{eq:prop-2nd-upper-bound}})\exp\big(-c_{\ref{eq:prop-2nd-upper-bound}}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big).

Proof sketch.

By definition of the backbone in (
5.8
), the backbone contains at least
k
vertices. The proof of Proposition
5.1
“merges” each component of size at least
k
with the backbone, with error probability given on the right-hand side of (
5.43
). Hence, the component containing the backbone is the only remaining component of size at least
k
.
∎

The next corollary follows from Lemma
5.2
.
It is not sharp but it yields a useful temporary estimate.

Corollary 5.11

(Lower bound on largest component)
.

Consider a supercritical interpolating KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
with parameters
\alpha>1
,
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
,
\sigma\geq 0
,
d\in\mathbb{N}
. For each
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{(1)}|\leq n(A\log(n))^{-1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big)\leq n^{-\delta}.

Proof.

If
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})
holds for some
k_{n}
, then the largest component
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
must have at least the size of the backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})
. Setting
k=k_{n}=(A\log(n))^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
in (
5.9
), the backbone exists with probability at least
1-n^{-\delta}
for
A=A(\delta)
sufficiently large, since
s_{k}=s_{k_{n}}=(C_{1}/16)A\log(n)
by (
5.7
). Then its size is at least
(n^{\prime}/k)s_{k}\geq(n^{\prime}/k)=\Theta(nk_{n}^{-1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
by definition of
n^{\prime}
in (
5.2
), finishing the proof.
∎

The next proposition shows that all vertices with a sufficiently high mark (simultaneously) belong to the largest component
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}
with polynomially small error probability.

Proposition
⋆
5.12

(Controlling marks of non-giant vertices)
.

Consider an i-KSRG in the setting of Theorem
2.2
(ii). For
all

\delta>0
, there exists
M_{\delta}>0
such that for
\overline{w}(n,\delta)=(M_{\delta}\log(n))^{(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}

(5.44)

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists u\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w}(n,\delta),\infty):u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq n^{-\delta}.

Proof sketch.

We give the detailed proof in the Appendix on page
A
, and here a sketch. We consider
k
as a free parameter, so using Lemma
5.2
with
k=k_{n}=\Theta\big(\log^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}(n)\big)
,
a backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})
exists and satisfies
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}
, with probability at least
1-n^{-\delta}
by Corollary
5.10
and Lemma
5.2
(the same calculation as the proof of Corollary
5.11
). The choice of
\overline{w}=\overline{w}(n,\delta)
is so that a vertex
u
with mark
w_{u}\geq\overline{w}(n,\delta)
connects to each backbone-vertex in its own subbox with probability at least
p
in (
2.3
). For
u
to not be contained in
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}}
, these
s_{k_{n}}
many edges must be all absent. Then we apply concentration inequalities to obtain the result.
∎

Remark 5.13
.

Combined with the proof of the lower bound of Theorem
2.4
below in Section
7
, one may show that Proposition
5.12
is sharp up to a constant factor, i.e., there exist constants
\delta,m_{w}>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large

\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[(m_{w}\log(n))^{(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\geq 1-n^{-\delta}.

We state a proposition that 0 is with constant probability in a linear-sized component in the induced subgraph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k))
with
k=k_{n}=\mathrm{polylog}(n)
and
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k)
from (
5.7
). Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)
be the component containing
0
in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}\subseteq{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
, by setting
{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)
to be the empty set if
w_{0}\geq 2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k)
.

Proposition 5.14

(Existence of a large component)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
, with parameters
\alpha>1
,
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
,
\sigma\geq 0
,
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
There exist constants
\rho,m>0
such that for all
n
sufficiently large, when
k=k_{n}=(m\log(n))^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
,

(5.45)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)|\geq\rho n\big)\geq\rho,\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}(0)|=\infty\big)\geq\rho.

Proof sketch.

For the first inequality in (
5.45
), we build a connected backbone on vertices with mark in
[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
using Lemma
5.2
. Then we use a second-moment method to show that the origin and linearly many other vertices are connected to this backbone via paths along which the vertex marks are increasing. The second statement follows similarly, forming an infinite path along which the marks are increasing.
The detailed proof can be found in Appendix
A.1
.
∎

6.
Upper bound: subexponential decay

In this section we prove Theorem
2.2
(ii).
We carry out the plan in Section
3.1.2
in detail. Instead of arguing directly for KSRGs with parameters described in Theorem
2.2
(ii), we derive general conditions that ensure that a bound on the size of the second-largest component as in Proposition
5.1
readily implies
subexponential decay.
Recall from Definition
2.1
that
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
denotes the conditional measure that
{\mathcal{V}}
contains a vertex at location
x\in\Lambda_{n}
, that has an unknown mark from distribution
F_{W}
.

Proposition 6.1

(Prerequisites for subexponential decay)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
, with parameters
\alpha>1,\tau>2
and
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Assume that there exist
\zeta,\eta,c,c^{\prime},\eta,M_{c}>0
, and a function
n_{0}(k)=O(k^{1+c^{\prime}})
, such that for all
k
sufficiently large constant and whenever
n\in[n_{0}(k),\infty)
, with

\overline{w}(n,c):=M_{c}\log^{\eta}(n)
it holds that for any
x\in\Lambda_{n}

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{c^{\prime}}\exp\big(-ck^{\zeta}\big),

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\leq n^{c}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-1-c},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w}(n,c),\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n^{-c}.

Then there exists a constant
A>0
such that for all
k
sufficiently large constant and
n
satisfying
n\in[n_{0}(k),\infty]
,

(6.4)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq A\exp\big(-(1/A)k^{\zeta}\big),

and

(6.5)

\frac{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|}{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}(0)|=\infty\big),\qquad\mbox{as $n\to\infty$.}

Observe that (
6.4
) does not follow from a naive application of (
6.1
), since we sharpened the polynomial prefactor on the right-hand side of (
6.1
) to a universal constant
A
in (
6.4
), and
n=\infty
is also allowed in (
6.4
). The inequalities (
6.1
)–(
6.3
) are satisfied when
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
and
\sigma\leq\tau-1
by Propositions
5.1
and
5.12
and Corollary
5.11
(we leave it to the reader to verify that the results hold also for the Palm-version
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
of
\mathbb{P}
). Thus, Theorem
2.2
(ii) follows immediately after we prove Proposition
6.1
. We state and prove an intermediate claim that we need for Proposition
6.1
. We write
{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
for the largest component in the graph induced on vertices in
{\mathcal{Q}}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.

Claim 6.2

(Leaving the giant)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
, under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
. Assume that (
6.1
)–(
6.3
) hold,
n
is sufficiently large and let
N\in[n,\infty]
. Then there exists
\delta>0
such that for any box
{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}\subseteq\Lambda_{N}
with
\mathrm{Vol}({\mathcal{Q}}_{n})=n
, any
x\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}
,

n
sufficiently large, and all
N\geq n
, it holds for
u:=(x,w_{u})
that

(6.6)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq n^{-\delta}.

Proof.

We will first prove the following bound that holds generally for a sequence of increasing graphs
G_{n}\subseteq G_{n+1}\subseteq\dots
, whose largest and second-largest components we denote by
C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
and
C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
, respectively.
Let
(k_{n})_{n\geq 0}
and
(K_{n})_{n\geq 0}
be two non-negative sequences such that
k_{n+1}<K_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
. Then, for all
0<n\leq N\leq\infty
,

(6.7)

\mathbb{P}\big(u\in C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\sum_{\tilde{n}=n}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<K_{\tilde{n}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k_{\tilde{n}}\big)\Big).

We verify the bound using an inductive argument.
We define for
\tilde{n}\geq n
the events

{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n}):=\big\{|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq K_{\tilde{n}}\big\}\cap\big\{|C_{\tilde{n}+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq k_{\tilde{n}+1}\big\}.

Since by assumption
k_{\tilde{n}+1}<K_{\tilde{n}}
, the event
{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n})
ensures that
|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
is already larger than
|C_{\tilde{n}+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
, hence
{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n})

implies that
C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\subseteq C_{\tilde{n}+1}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
.
Iteratively applying this argument yields that
\cap_{\tilde{n}\in[n,N]}{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n})
implies that
\{C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\subseteq C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
. We combine this with the observation that given that
u\in V_{n}
, it holds that
\{u\in C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\subseteq\{C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\nsubseteq C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
. This yields

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(u\in C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\nsubseteq C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\bigg(\{C_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\nsubseteq C_{N}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\cap\bigcap_{\tilde{n}=n}^{N-1}{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n})\bigg)+\sum_{\tilde{n}=n}^{N-1}\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}(\tilde{n})\big)

\displaystyle\leq 0+\sum_{\tilde{n}=n}^{N}\Big(\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<K_{\tilde{n}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(|C_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k_{\tilde{n}}\big)\Big),

showing (
6.7
). We move on to (
6.6
) for which we have to define the increasing sequence of graphs. Consider any sequence of boxes
({\mathcal{Q}}_{\tilde{n}})_{\tilde{n}\geq n}
such that
{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}\subseteq{\mathcal{Q}}_{n+1}\subseteq\dots\subseteq{\mathcal{Q}}_{N}:=\Lambda_{N}
and
\mathrm{Vol}({\mathcal{Q}}_{\tilde{n}})=\tilde{n}
, and let
G_{\tilde{n}}
denote the induced subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}
on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tilde{n}}
for
\tilde{n}\in[n,N]
, that is conditioned to contain a vertex at location
x\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{n}
.
Using the assumed lower bound on
|\mathcal{C}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|
in (
6.2
), we set
K_{\tilde{n}}:=\tilde{n}^{c}
, and using the assumed upper bound on
|{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|
in (
6.1
), there exists a large constant
A
so that whenever
n
is sufficiently large, and
\tilde{n}\geq n
, by setting
k_{\tilde{n}}:=(A\log(\tilde{n}))^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
, it holds that

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tilde{n}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<K_{\tilde{n}}\big)\leq\tilde{n}^{-c-1},\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{\tilde{n}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>k_{\tilde{n}}\big)\leq\tilde{n}^{-c-1}.

Clearly
k_{\tilde{n}+1}<K_{\tilde{n}}
for
\tilde{n}\geq n
, so that substituting the bounds into (
6.8
) and summing over
\tilde{n}\geq n
yields the assertion (
6.6
) for any
\delta<c
and
n
sufficiently large.
∎

We continue to prove Proposition
6.1
, starting with some notation.
For some
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\})
, and using
c,c^{\prime},\eta,\zeta
, and
\overline{w}(n,c)
from the statement of Proposition
6.1

(6.9)

\displaystyle N_{k}

\displaystyle:=\exp(k^{\zeta}c/(2c^{\prime})),

\displaystyle n_{k}

\displaystyle:=N_{k}^{\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\overline{w}_{N_{k}}

\displaystyle:=\overline{w}(N_{k},c)=M_{c}(k^{\zeta})^{\eta}(\tfrac{c}{2c^{\prime}})^{\eta},

\displaystyle t_{k}

\displaystyle:=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k).

Note that
N_{k},n_{k}=\exp(\Theta(k^{\zeta}))
.
For
n\leq N_{k}
, the statement (
6.4
) follows directly from (
6.1
), since when
n=N_{k}
, the right-hand side of (
6.1
) becomes
\exp(-k^{\zeta}c/2)
, so we may set any
A
such that
1/A\leq c/2
in (
6.4
). So we may assume
in the remainder of the section that
n>N_{k}
.
We write for
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n)}(u)
the component of vertex
u:=(x,w_{u})\in{\mathcal{V}}
in the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{\infty}
restricted to
\Lambda(x,n)
. Define for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
the two events

(6.10)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})

\displaystyle:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k,\exists v_{1},v_{2}\in\Xi_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mbox{ such that }\\
&v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u),\|x_{v_{1}}-x_{v_{2}}\|\geq t_{k},\mbox{ and }v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}\end{aligned}\right\},

(6.11)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})

\displaystyle:=\big\{\exists v_{1}\in\Xi_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}}),\exists v_{2}\in\Xi\setminus\Xi_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})},v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}\big\}.

The next lemma relates the probability of the event
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
to the events
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}
