using the assumed bounds in Proposition
6.1
.

Lemma 6.3

(Extending the box-sizes)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model as in Definition
2.1
, under the same setting as Proposition
6.1
. Assume that (
6.1
)–(
6.3
) hold.
Then there exists
A^{\prime}>0
such that for all
n
with
n\in[N_{k},\infty]
, whenever
u=(x,w_{u})\in{\mathcal{V}}
is a vertex with
\|x-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|\geq N_{k}^{1/d}/2
,

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|

\displaystyle\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip-20.0pt\leq A^{\prime}\exp\big(-(1/A^{\prime})k^{\zeta}\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\big).

Proof.

Let
\tilde{n}\geq 1
, and denote by
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,\tilde{n})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
the largest connected component in the induced subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\infty}
inside the box
\Lambda(x,\tilde{n})\subseteq\Lambda_{n}
. For a vertex
u=(x,w_{u})\in{\mathcal{V}}
define

(6.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,\tilde{n})

\displaystyle:=\{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,\tilde{n})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\},

(6.14)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})

\displaystyle:=\big\{\forall v\in\Xi_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}[\overline{w}_{N_{k}},\infty):v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big\}.

The first events relates to (
6.6
) in Claim
6.2
, while the second one to (
6.3
) of Proposition
6.1
.
The values of
n_{k}<N_{k}\leq n
from (
6.9
) and that we assumed
\|x-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|\geq N_{k}^{1/d}/2
ensure that
\Lambda(x,n_{k})\!\subseteq\!\Lambda(x,N_{k})\!\subseteq\!\Lambda_{n}
. Then we bound

\displaystyle\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k

\displaystyle,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big\}

\displaystyle\subseteq\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big\}\,\cup\,\big\{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\cup\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|<k\big\}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\subseteq\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big\}\,\cup\,{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,n_{k})\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\cup\,\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k\big\}.\end{aligned}

Applying probabilities on both sides we obtain the inequality stated in (
3.6
) for
x\!=\!0
.
We introduce a shorthand notation for the third event on the right-hand side of (
6.15
), i.e.,

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}}:=\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k\big\}

Define the auxiliary events

(6.16)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}

\displaystyle:=\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k,|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{outof-giant}}(n_{k},n)

\displaystyle:=\{u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\},

and observe that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}}={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{outof-giant}}(n_{k},n).

In order to bound
\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}})
, we distinguish whether
u
enters the giant at the intermediate box of size
N_{k}\in(n_{k},n)
or not:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}}

\displaystyle\subseteq\{u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\cup\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k\}

(6.17)

\displaystyle={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,N_{k})\cup\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{outof-giant}}(n_{k},N_{k})\big),

with
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,\tilde{n})
defined in (
6.14
).
We observe that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}

in (
6.16
) implies that at least one of the at most
k-1
vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)
has an incident edge crossing the boundary of
\Lambda(x,n_{k})
, and so there must exist a “fairly” long edge either inside the cluster
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)
or between a vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)
and a vertex in
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)
. More precisely, recalling
t_{k}=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k)
, define

(6.18)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}:=\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)|<k,\exists v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u),v_{2}\in\Xi:v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2},\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|\geq t_{k}\big\}.

We argue that
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}
. By the pigeon-hole principle, if all edges adjacent to all vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}(u)
were shorter than
t_{k}
, the furthest point that could be reached from
x
with at most
k-1
edges has Euclidean norm at most
(k-1)t_{k}<n_{k}^{1/d}/2
, and thus its location would be inside
\Lambda(x,n_{k})
, contradicting the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{becomes-large}}
in (
6.16
). Returning to (
6.17
), we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}}

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,N_{k})\cup\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{outof-giant}}(n_{k},N_{k})\big)

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,N_{k})\cup\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}\cap\{u\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}\}\big).

In order to bound the existence of long edges, we put restrictions on the marks: we distinguish whether all vertices in
\Xi_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}\setminus{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
have mark at most
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
– this is the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})
in (
6.14
) – or not.
We obtain

(6.19)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{goal}}

\displaystyle\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,N_{k})\cup\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w})\big)

\displaystyle\cup\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}\cap\{u\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w})\big).

The intersection with
\{u\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})
in the last event ensures that
all
vertices in the cluster of
u
with location in
\Lambda(x,N_{k})\supseteq\Lambda(x,n_{k})
have mark at most
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
. We make another case distinction, with respect to the locations of the vertices the edge
e_{\geq t_{k}}
of length at least
t_{k}
that exists on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}
in (
6.18
). Namely,
e_{\geq t_{k}}
either has both endpoints in
\Lambda_{N_{k}}
or it has one endpoint inside
\Lambda_{n_{k}}
and the other one outside
\Lambda_{N_{k}}
. For the first event, we obtain the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{k})
, and for the latter
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{k})
, respectively (defined in (
6.10
)—(
6.11
)).
Hence,

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{edge}}\cap\{u\notin{\mathcal{C}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}_{\Lambda(x,N_{k})}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\subseteq{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{k})\cup{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{k}).

Using this in (
6.19
), then substituting (
6.19
) back into (
6.15
), and then taking probabilities yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k

\displaystyle,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,n_{k})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(x,N_{k},\overline{w})\big)+\sum_{\tilde{n}\in\{n_{k},N_{k}\}}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,\tilde{n})\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(x,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\big).

The event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{leave-giant}}(x,\tilde{n})\!=\!\{u\!\in\!{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,\tilde{n})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\!\notin\!{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
considers the graph in the box
\Lambda_{n}
(which is centered at the origin) and therefore does not necessarily have the same probability for all
x\in\Lambda_{n}
. The four other events consider the graph in boxes centered at
x
. Hence, we translate those events (and the Palm measure
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
) by
-x
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k

\displaystyle,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(0,N_{k},\overline{w})\big)+\sum_{\tilde{n}\in\{n_{k},N_{k}\}}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}\big(u\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\Lambda(x,\tilde{n})}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\big)+\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\big),

The first two terms can be bounded by substituting the definitions
n_{k},N_{k}=\exp\big(\Theta(k^{\zeta})\big)
in (
6.9
) into the assumed bounds on
the probabilities
in Proposition
6.1
.
The terms in the sum are bounded from above by
n_{k}^{-\delta}=\exp\big(-\Theta(k^{\zeta})\big)
by Claim
6.2
.
This finishes the proof of (
6.12
).
∎

We move on to bounding
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}})
on the right-hand side of (
6.12
) in Proposition
6.3
, with
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}
from (
6.10
). To do so, we need an auxiliary claim that controls the probability that for every point in
\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})
there are not “too many” points at distance at least
t_{k}
, with
t_{k}=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k)
.
We define first for
i\geq 1
,
and
u=(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[0,\overline{w})
the annuli

(6.20)

{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u}):=\big(\Lambda\big(x_{u},(2^{i}t_{k})^{d}\big)\setminus\Lambda\big(x_{u},(2^{i-1}t_{k})^{d}\big)\big)\times[1,\infty).

With the measure
\mu_{\tau}
from (
2.2
), we then define the
bad
events

(6.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}

\displaystyle:=\big\{\exists i\geq 1,u\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}}):|\Xi_{N_{k}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u})|>2\cdot\mu_{\tau}\big({\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u})\big)\big\}.

In the following auxiliary claim we give an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)
. Its proof is standard, based on Palm theory and Chernoff bounds, see page
B
of Appendix
B
.

Claim 6.4
.

For all
c,\delta>0
, there exists
n_{0}
such that for all
n_{k}\geq\max\{n_{0},k^{d+\delta}\}
,

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)\leq n_{k}^{-c}
.

We can now analyze
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}})
in Lemma
6.3
. The next claim holds for the specific choices of
n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
and
t_{k}
in (
6.9
) for all
k
is sufficiently large, but extends to more general settings, e.g. KSRGs defined on any vertex set that satisfies
\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}
in (
6.21
).

Claim 6.5

(No
low
-mark edge from a small component)
.

Consider an i-KSRG that satisfies the conditions in Proposition
6.1
. For any
\varepsilon\in(0,\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\})
in (
6.9
), there exists a constant
A^{\prime}>0
, such that for
k
sufficiently large

(6.22)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\mid\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)\leq\begin{cases}A^{\prime}\exp(-(1/A^{\prime})k^{\zeta}),&\text{if }\alpha<\infty,\\
0,&\text{if }\alpha=\infty.\end{cases}

Proof of Claim
6.5
.

Assume first
\alpha=\infty
. The event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})
is by definition in (
6.10
) restricted to vertices of mark at most
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}=\overline{w}(N_{k},c)
in (
6.9
). We write
t_{k}
in (
6.9
) as
t_{k}=\exp((\varepsilon/d)k^{\zeta})/(2k)
, which is larger than
\beta\overline{w}_{N_{k}}^{1+\sigma}=\beta M_{c}^{1+\sigma}((c/(2c^{\prime}))k^{\zeta})^{\eta(\sigma+1)}
for
k
sufficiently large.
Hence, the indicator in
\mathrm{p}(u,v)
is then
0
by (
2.3
), so a connection between
u,v
can not occur.

Assume
\alpha<\infty
.
To obtain an upper bound on the left-hand side of (
6.22
), we condition on the full realization
\Xi
satisfying the event
\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}
containing
0
:

(6.23)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w})\mid\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)=\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big[\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mid\Xi,\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)\big].

Let us denote the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{k}}
with edges of length at most
t_{k}=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k)
by
{\mathcal{G}}_{n_{k}}(\leq t_{k})
and write
{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})
for the component in this graph containing the origin.
Clearly,

\left\{\begin{aligned} \hskip-4.0pt&|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0)|<k,\exists v_{1},v_{2}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mbox{ s.t. }\\
&v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0),\|x_{v_{1}}-x_{v_{2}}\|\geq t_{k},\mbox{ and }v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}\end{aligned}\right\}\hskip-2.0pt\subseteq\hskip-2.0pt\left\{\begin{aligned} &|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})|<k,\exists v_{1},v_{2}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mbox{ s.t. }\\
&v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k}),\|x_{v_{1}}-x_{v_{2}}\|\geq t_{k},\mbox{ and }v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}\end{aligned}\hskip-2.0pt\right\},

where the left-hand side is the definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}
in (
6.10
).
Conditionally on
\Xi
, all edges of length at least
t_{k}
are present independently of edges shorter than
t_{k}
. We obtain
by a union bound over all vertices in
\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\subseteq\Xi
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}

\displaystyle\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mid\Xi,\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)

(6.24)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v_{1}\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k}),|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})|<k\mid\Xi,\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{2}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}}):\\
\|x_{v_{1}}-x_{v_{2}}\|\geq t_{k}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{p}(v_{1},v_{2})}_{:=T(v_{1})}.

Using the definitions of
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
),
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
from (
1.10
), the upper bound on
|{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(u)|
in
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}
in (
6.21
), mark bounds
w_{v_{1}},w_{v_{2}}\leq\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
, and the distance bound
\|x_{v_{1}}-x_{v_{2}}\|\geq 2^{(i-1)}t_{k}
when
x_{v_{2}}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u})
in (
6.20
),
the following bound holds uniformly for all

v_{1}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{k})
:

\displaystyle T(v_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{2}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{k})\\
v_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(v_{1})\end{subarray}}\mathrm{p}(v_{1},v_{2})\leq\sum_{i\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v_{2}\in\Xi_{N_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{k})\\
v_{2}\in{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(v_{1})\end{subarray}}p\beta^{\alpha}\kappa_{1,\sigma}^{\alpha}(\overline{w}_{N_{k}},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})2^{-(i-1)\alpha d}t_{k}^{-\alpha d}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{i\geq 1}t_{k}^{d}(2^{id}-2^{(i-1)d})p\beta^{\alpha}\kappa_{1,\sigma}^{\alpha}(\overline{w}_{N_{k}},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})2^{-(i-1)\alpha d}t_{k}^{-\alpha d}

(6.25)

\displaystyle=2(2^{d}-1)p\beta^{\alpha}\overline{w}_{N_{k}}^{\alpha(\sigma+1)}t_{k}^{(1-\alpha)d}\sum_{i\geq 1}2^{-(\alpha-1)(i-1)d}.

Since
\alpha>1
by assumption in Theorem
2.2
, the sum on the right-hand side is finite. This gives a bound on
T(v_{1})
in (
6.24
) that does not depend on
v_{1}
.
Hence, returning to (
6.24
),

\displaystyle\sum_{v_{1}\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(v_{1}

\displaystyle\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k}),|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})|<k\mid\Xi,\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\Bigg[\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})|<k\}}\sum_{v_{1}\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}_{N_{k}})}\mathbbm{1}_{\{v_{1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})\}}\mid\Xi,\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\Bigg]<k,

since on realizations of the graph satisfying
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{n_{k}}(0,\leq t_{k})\leq k\}
, the sum that follows is at most
k-1
. Substituting this with (
6.25
) into (
6.24
) yields for
(
6.23
),
that for some constant
C>0

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{low-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\mid\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)\leq C\,k\,\overline{w}_{N_{k}}^{\alpha(\sigma+1)}\,t_{k}^{(1-\alpha)d}.

Substituting on the right-hand side the choices of
t_{k}=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k)
, and
n_{k}=\exp(\varepsilon(c/2)k^{\zeta})
from (
6.9
), yield (
6.22
) for any
\varepsilon>0

(using
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
is polynomial in
k
).
∎

The last claim bounds the last term in Lemma
6.3
.
Recall
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}
from (
6.11
).

Claim 6.6

(No
long
edge from a small component)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model in Definition
2.1
with parameters
\alpha>1,\tau>2
and
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
Assume
N\geq n\geq 1
, and
\overline{w}\geq 1
such that

(6.26)

(N^{1/d}-n^{1/d})/2\geq\max\big\{\sqrt{d}n^{1/d},(\beta\overline{w}^{1+\sigma})^{1/d},N^{1/d}/4\big\}.

There exists a constant
C_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}>0
such that

(6.27)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\exists v_{1}\in\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w}),\exists v_{2}\in\Xi\setminus\Xi_{N}:v_{1}\leftrightarrow v_{2}\big)\leq C_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}\overline{w}^{c_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}}nN^{-\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\}}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha=\tau-1\}}\log(N)).

In particular, for
n_{k}
,
N_{k}
,
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
as in (
6.9
), if
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(M_{c},\eta)\in(0,\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\})
is sufficiently small, then for
k
sufficiently large, with
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}
defined in (
6.11
),

(6.28)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n_{k},N_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}})\big)\leq\exp\big(-\varepsilon k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big).

Proof.

We defer the proof of (
6.27
) (based on a first-moment method) to Appendix
B
on page
B
.
The bound (
6.28
) follows directly from (
6.27
) by substituting
n_{k}
,
N_{k}
and
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
from (
6.9
) to (
6.27
), then using that
\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
and
\log(N_{k})
are polynomial in
k
and of much smaller order than
n_{k}
and
N_{k}
.
∎

Having bounded all terms on the right-hand side in (
6.12
), we finish the section:

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

For
n\leq N_{k}
, using that
N_{k}=\exp\big((c/2)k^{\zeta}\big)
,
Proposition
6.1
follows directly from (
6.1
), since

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\geq k\big)

\displaystyle\leq N_{k}\exp\big(-ck^{\zeta}\big)=\exp\big(-(c/2)k^{\zeta}\big).

We now consider
n>N_{k}
.
Recall the values of
n_{k},\overline{w}_{N_{k}}
, and
t_{k}
from (
6.9
). Lemma
6.3
and Claims
6.4
–
6.6
directly imply (
6.4
) in Proposition
6.1
.
We will now prove the law of large numbers (
6.5
). In
[
35
]
it is shown that finite i-KSRGs
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}=({\mathcal{V}}_{n},{\mathcal{E}}_{n})
rooted at a vertex at the origin (see Definition
2.1
) converge locally to their infinite rooted version
({\mathcal{G}}_{\infty},0)
as
n\to\infty
. We refer to
[
34
]
and its references for an introduction to local limits. We use the concept of local limits as a black box and verify a necessary and sufficient condition for the law of large numbers for the size of the giant component for graphs that have a local limit by Van der Hofstad
[
33
, Theorem 2.2]
.
We state the condition: let
({\mathcal{G}}_{n})_{n\geq 1}
be a sequence of graphs with
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|=n
that converges locally in probability to
({\mathcal{G}}_{\infty},\varnothing)
(here
\varnothing
denotes the root of the graph). Assume that

(6.29)

\lim_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,\,|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)|\geq k,\,{\mathcal{C}}(u)\neq{\mathcal{C}}(v)\}}\Big]=0,

then, as
n\to\infty
,

\frac{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|}{n}\overset{\mathbb{P}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\infty}(\varnothing)|=\infty\big).

To verify condition (
6.29
), we have to consider a model satisfying
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|=n
. Therefore, we consider i-KSRGs on
\Lambda_{n}
that are conditioned to have
|{\mathcal{V}}_{n}|=n
. All our previous results extend to this model, as remarked in Section
3.3
. We analyze the indicator random variables in (
6.29
) for a fixed pair
u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}
. We distinguish whether one of them is part of the largest component to obtain

\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)|\geq k,{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)\neq{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)\}\subseteq\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\cup\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)|\geq k,v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}.

Using linearity of expectation over the
n^{2}
vertex pairs in the sum of (
6.29
), and that all
n
vertices are identically distributed, we obtain that

\lim_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u,v\in{\mathcal{V}}_{n}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\geq k,\,|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(v)|\geq k,\,{\mathcal{C}}(u)\neq{\mathcal{C}}(v)\}}\Big]\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}2\,\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(U)|\geq k,U\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big),

where
U
is a vertex with a uniform location
X\in\Lambda_{n}
and mark
w_{u}
following distribution
F_{W}
.
We condition on the location
X=x
, to obtain that

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(U)|\geq k,U\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq\frac{1}{n}\int_{x\in\Lambda_{n}:\|x-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|\geq N_{k}^{1/d}/2}\!\!\!\!\mathbb{P}^{x}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(u)|\!\geq\!k,u\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\mathrm{d}x+\mathbb{P}\big(\|X-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|\!<\!N_{k}^{1/d}/2\big).

The second term tends to zero as
n\to\infty
, while the first term is of order
\exp\big(-\Omega(k^{\zeta})\big)
by Lemma
6.3
and Claims
6.4
–
6.6
that apply for all
n\geq N_{k}
and
x
such that
\|x-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|\geq N_{k}^{1/d}/2
. Thus, the first term tends to zero as
k\to\infty
. This finishes the proof of the condition (
6.29
). The law of large numbers (
6.5
) follows.
∎

7.
Lower bounds

In this section we prove Theorem
2.2
(i). We first show that certain prerequisite inequalities imply the lower bound on the cluster-size decay, and afterwards we verify these inequalities for
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
. Eventually, we prove Theorem
2.5
.
We define for some constant
\eta>0
the function and set

(7.1)

\displaystyle w_{\eta}(\ell)

\displaystyle:=(\log(\ell))^{\eta},\qquad\Lambda_{\ell,\eta}:=\Lambda_{\ell}\times[1,w_{\eta}(\ell)),

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0)

\displaystyle:=\mbox{the component containing $(0,w_{0})$ in }{\mathcal{G}}_{\ell}[1,w_{\eta}(\ell)),

i.e.,
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0)
is the component of
(0,w_{0})
in the graph induced on vertices in
\Lambda_{\ell,\eta}
– and let
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0):=\emptyset
if
w_{0}\geq w_{\eta}(\ell)
. We recall
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Z}})
, the multiplicity of the maximum of
{\mathcal{Z}}
, from (
2.5
); and the exponents
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}}
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
from (
1.7
), (
1.8
), (
1.12
), and (
1.9
). Define for some small constant
\varepsilon>0
to be defined later, and
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}

(7.2)

\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}:=\begin{cases}\big(\varepsilon\log(n)/(\log\log(n))^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}\big)^{1/\max({\mathcal{Z}})},&\text{if }\max({\mathcal{Z}})>0,\\
\exp\big((\varepsilon\log(n))^{1/(\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1)}\big),&\text{if }\max({\mathcal{Z}})=0,\mbox{ and }\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}>1.\end{cases}

Note that
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}=\tfrac{d-1}{d}
, which is equal to 0 only if
d=1
, and positive otherwise. Thus, only in dimension
d=1
one can have
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
different from
\Theta(\mathrm{polylog}(n))
. More precisely, for
d=1
,
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}=\Theta(\mathrm{poly}(n))
if only one out of
\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
is zero, and the others are negative; and it is stretched exponential in the logarithm if at least two elements out of
\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
are zero, and none of them is positive. If the three values
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
are all negative, then the model is always subcritical when
d=1
as shown by Gracar, Lüchtrath, and Mönch
[
27
]
.

By an “i-KSRG on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
” we mean the model mentioned in Section
3.3
, i.e., when
\Xi
is replaced by vertices in
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
having iid marks from distribution
F_{W}
in (
2.2
) in Definition
2.1
.

Proposition 7.1

(Lower bound holds when linear-sized giant on truncated weights exists)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model in Definition
2.1
with parameters
\alpha\in(1,\infty]
,
\tau\in(2,\infty]
,
\sigma\geq 0
,
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
, or a supercritical i-KSRG on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
with additionally
\min\{p,p\beta^{\alpha}\}<1
in (
2.3
). Assume that there exist constants
\eta,\rho>0
such that for all
\ell
sufficiently large, and with
{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0)
from (
7.1
),

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0)|\geq\rho\ell\big)\geq\rho.

Then there exists
A>0
such that for all
n\in[Ak,\infty]
, with
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
,

(7.4)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\geq\exp\big(-Ak^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(k)\big).

Moreover, there exists
\delta,\varepsilon>0
, such that for all
n
sufficiently large, with
k_{n,\varepsilon}
from (
7.2
),

(7.5)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|>\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}\big)\geq 1-n^{-\delta}.

By Proposition
5.14
, the condition (
7.3
) is satisfied when
\tau\in(2,2+\sigma)
, implying Theorem
2.2
(i). In
[
43
]
, we show that (
7.3
) holds whenever
\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}>0
and the model is supercritical.
We prove Proposition
7.1
by formalizing the reasoning at the beginning of Section
1.2
. First, we assume that the vertex set is given by the PPP
\Xi
in Definition
2.1
. Then we explain how to extend to i-KSRGs on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.

7.1.
Finding a localized component

The goal of this section is to prove Proposition
7.1
.
First we aim to bound the probability of
\{{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
in (
7.4
) from below. To do so, we write it as the intersection of “almost independent” events, for which we introduce some notation now:

Two large components.

We write
\Lambda(x,s)=\Lambda_{s}(x)
for a box of volume
s
centered at
x
, see (
2.1
). We define for a constant
M_{\mathrm{in}}>0
the radius and Euclidean ball

(7.6)

r_{k}:=(kM_{\mathrm{in}})^{1/d},\qquad{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}:=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}:\|x\|\leq r_{k}\},

where
M_{\mathrm{in}}>1/\rho
is implicitly defined given
\rho
from Proposition
7.1
, such that
\|\partial\Lambda_{k/\rho}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|=r_{k}/2
, implying also that
\Lambda_{k/\rho}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
. We would like to constrain
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)
to the ball
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, and we would like to find a component outside
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
that is larger than
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|
.
We ‘construct’ these two components, with the constant
M_{\mathrm{out}}:=2^{d+2}M_{\mathrm{in}}
, on vertices in the (hyper)rectangles (see also (
7.1
))

(7.7)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle:=\Lambda_{k/\rho,\eta}=\Lambda(0,k/\rho)\times\big[1,w_{\eta}(k/\rho)\big),\quad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}:=\Lambda(x_{\mathrm{out}},kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho)\times\big[1,w_{\eta}(kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho)\big),

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle:=\Lambda(0,k/\rho),\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathrm{out}}:=\Lambda(x_{\mathrm{out}},(kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho)),

where
x_{\mathrm{out}}:=(x^{\mathrm{out}}_{1},0,\dots,0)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
is defined implicitly given
\rho
such that
\|\partial\Lambda(x_{\mathrm{out}},kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho)-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|:=r_{k}/2
.
We assume that
n
is sufficiently large so that
\Lambda_{\mathrm{in}}\cup\Lambda_{\mathrm{out}}\subseteq\Lambda_{n}
.
Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)
be the component of
(0,w_{0})
in the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
induced on vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)=\emptyset
if
w_{0}\geq w_{\eta}(k/\rho)
. Since
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, it is immediate that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)\subseteq\Xi_{{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}}
. Let
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
be the largest component in the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
induced on vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
.
Define the events

(7.8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{ingiant}}

\displaystyle:=\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\},\qquad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{outgiant}}

\displaystyle:=\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq kM_{\mathrm{out}}\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}

\displaystyle:=\{|\Xi_{{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}}|\leq kM_{\mathrm{out}}/2\},\qquad

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{ingiant}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{outgiant}}.

Figure 3
.
A visualization of the
\gamma
-suppressed profile
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
.
The horizontal axis represents space, the vertical axis represents marks.
\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}
demands no vertices in the yellow region.
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}(\gamma)
demands that there is no edge between vertices in the inner and vertices in the outer blue region,
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{components}}
requires that the two red areas contain large components;
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
ensures that
\Xi
is ‘close to being typical’ in the red areas
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
.

Isolation

On
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}
,
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)\supseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)
could still connect to the giant/infinite component.
To prevent this, we will ban edges that cross the boundary of
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
as follows.
In Section
3.2
we informally described an “optimally-suppressed mark-profile”. We first define a suppressed mark-profile, then optimize its shape. Let
\gamma\in(0,1/(\sigma+1)]
be a constant.
We define, for
C_{\beta}:=(2\beta)^{1/d}
, and for
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
\|x-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|=|\|x\|-r_{k}|=:z
, the
\gamma
-suppressed profile by

(7.9)

\displaystyle f_{\gamma}(z)

\displaystyle:=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }z\leq C_{\beta},\\
(z/C_{\beta})^{\gamma d}&\text{if }z\in(C_{\beta},r_{k}],\\
(z/C_{\beta})^{d}(r_{k}/C_{\beta})^{-d(1-\gamma)}&\text{if }z>r_{k},\end{cases}

(7.10)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle:=\{(x_{v},f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)):x_{v}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\}.

We say that
v
is
below, on, or above

{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
if
w_{v}
is at most, equal to, or strictly larger than
f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)
, respectively. We say
\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
if all points in
\Xi
are below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
, see Figure
3
. We split the PPP
\Xi
into four independent PPPs, whether points fall below or above
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
, and inside or outside
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
:

(7.11)

\displaystyle\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle:=\{(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi:x_{u}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{u}\leq f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{u}\|-r_{k}|)\},

\displaystyle\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle:=\{(x_{v},w_{v})\in\Xi:x_{v}\notin{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{v}\leq f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)\},

\displaystyle\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}

\displaystyle:=\{(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi:x_{u}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{u}>f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{u}\|-r_{k}|)\},

\displaystyle\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}

\displaystyle:=\{(x_{v},w_{v})\in\Xi:x_{v}\notin{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}},w_{v}>f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)\}.

For
A,B\subseteq\Xi
we denote by
|{\mathcal{E}}(A,B)|
the number of edges between vertices in
A
and
B
. Define

(7.12)

\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}=\{|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|=0\},\quad{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{noedge}}(\gamma):=\{|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}})|=0\}.

Under
\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{noedge}}(\gamma)
, the vertices in
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
are not connected to the unique infinite component when
n=\infty
and are isolated from the rest of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
when
n<\infty
.
We comment on the profile function
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
).
The event
\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}
demands no vertices within distance
C_{\beta}
from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{M_{\mathrm{in}}}
, since
f_{\gamma}(|\|x\|-r_{k}|)=1
for
\|x-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|\leq C_{\beta}
, and vertex marks are above
1
a.s. The function
f_{\gamma}
is continuous and increasing in
z
: the closer a point
x
is to the boundary of
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, the stronger the restriction on its mark in
\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
. This is natural since vertices with higher mark close to
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
are more likely to have an edge crossing this boundary, which we want to prevent. While the event
\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}
becomes less likely when
\gamma
is small,
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{noedge}}(\gamma)
becomes more likely. This naturally leads to an optimization problem, that we set up next.
Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{0,\alpha-(\tau-1),(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\}
, and define

(7.13)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}&:=\begin{cases}\max_{0<\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)}\big\{\gamma:1-\gamma(\tau-1)\overset{(\star)}{\geq}2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})\},&\text{if }\alpha<\infty,\\
1/(\sigma+1),&\text{if }\alpha=\infty,\end{cases}\\
\qquad\zeta_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}&:=1-\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}(\tau-1),\end{aligned}

The inequality
(\star)
holds for
\gamma=0
since
\alpha>1
by assumption, therefore
\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}>0
and is well-defined.
We call
f_{\star}:=f_{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}
the
optimally-suppressed mark-profile
(and show below that it is indeed “optimal”) and write
{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}:={\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}
. We define the isolation event using the events in (
7.12
), see also Figure
3
:

(7.14)

{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}:=\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{noedge}}(\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}).

Ensuring almost independence

The events
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}
and
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\}
in (
7.8
) are correlated, since
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\}
may push up the number of high-mark vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
, making
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}
less likely.
To overcome the dependence, we introduce two auxiliary events that ensure regularity of
\Xi
in the hyperrectangles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
from (
7.7
).
Using
w_{\eta}
from (
7.1
), define, using
c_{\mathrm{in}}:=1/\rho,c_{\mathrm{out}}:=M_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho
:

(7.15)

\displaystyle j^{\star}_{\mathrm{in}}:=\min\{j\in{\mathbb{N}}:w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{in}})/2^{j}<1\},\quad j^{\star}_{\mathrm{out}}:=\min\{j\in{\mathbb{N}}:w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{out}})/2^{j}<1)\}.

and writing
\mathrm{loc}\in\{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{out}\}
, define

(7.16)

I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}}:=[w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{loc}})/2^{j},w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{loc}})/2^{j-1}),\qquad 1\leq j<j^{\star}_{\mathrm{loc}}.

Using
\Lambda_{\mathrm{in}},\Lambda_{\mathrm{out}}
in (
7.7
), the intensity measure
\mu_{\tau}
of
\Xi
in (
2.2
), and
\Xi_{\mathrm{loc}}(I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}})
for the vertices in
\Xi\cap(\Lambda_{\mathrm{loc}}\times I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}})
, consider the following events (see also Figure
3
) for
\mathrm{loc}\in\{\mathrm{in},\mathrm{out}\}
:

(7.17)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{loc})}(\eta)

\displaystyle:=\{\forall j\leq j^{\star}_{\mathrm{loc}}:|\Xi_{\mathrm{loc}}(I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}})|\leq 2\mu_{\tau}(\Lambda_{\mathrm{loc}}\times I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}})\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}({\eta})

\displaystyle:={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{in})}({\eta})\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{out})}({\eta}).

Finally, fix a realization of the induced subgraphs
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}=(\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}},{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}))\cup(\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}))
so that the vertex set
\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
satisfies the event
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
for some
\eta>0
, and define the conditional probability measure and expectation by

(7.18)

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\,\cdot\,\big):=\mathbb{P}\big(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big),\qquad\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}[\,\cdot\,]:=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big]

In the conditioning we reveal both the vertex and edge sets in the disjoint boxes
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}.
The event
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
checks the number of vertices in hyperrectangles inside
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
, hence
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
is measurable with respect to
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}},{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
and in principle can be left out of the conditioning in (
7.18
).
We state a lemma that will imply Proposition
7.1
.

Lemma 7.2

(Lower bound for isolation)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG model in Definition
2.1
with parameters
\alpha\in(1,\infty]
,
\tau\in(2,\infty]
,
\sigma\geq 0
, and
d\in{\mathbb{N}}
, or a supercritical i-KSRG on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
with additionally
p<1
in (
2.3
).
For any constant
\eta>0
in (
7.1
), there exists
A>0
, such that for any realization of
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
that satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
, with
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
, with
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
from (
7.18
)

(7.19)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\big)\geq\exp\big(-Ar_{k}^{d\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(r_{k})\big).

The same bound holds for the Palm-version
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}
of
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
.

We now show that Proposition
7.1
follows directly from Lemma
7.2
.

Proof of Proposition
7.1
, assuming Lemma
7.2
.

We first show (
7.4
). Recall
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}
from (
7.8
), and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}({\eta})
from (
7.14
), (
7.17
) respectively. The intersection of these events implies
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}
, since
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\geq|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k
, and
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}
ensures that
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)
is fully contained in
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
. Hence,
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)|\leq|\Xi_{{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}}|\leq kM_{\mathrm{out}}/2
and
|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}|\geq kM_{\mathrm{out}}
ensure that
{\mathcal{C}}_{n}(0)
is not the largest component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
.
So, by the law of total probability

(7.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}

\displaystyle(0)|\geq k,0\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)-\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)-\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\big).

Recall
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{insmall}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}}=\{|\Xi_{{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}}|\leq kM_{\mathrm{out}}/2\}
from (
7.8
), and
M_{\mathrm{out}}=2^{d+2}M_{\mathrm{in}}
above (
7.7
). The box with side-length
2r_{k}=2(kM_{\mathrm{in}})^{1/d}
(by definition in (
7.6
)) centered at the origin is the smallest box that contains
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
. Using the intensity measure
\mu_{\tau}
from (
2.2
), and writing
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq}:=\{(x,w_{x})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}:x\in{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}};(x,w_{v})\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}({\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\leq})\leq 2^{d}kM_{\mathrm{in}}=2^{d+2}kM_{\mathrm{in}}/4=kM_{\mathrm{out}}/4.

By a standard concentration inequality for Poisson random variables (see Lemma
C.1
for
x=2
), there exist
c^{\prime},c>0
such that, since
r_{k}=\Theta(k^{1/d})
,

(7.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\big)\leq\exp(-c^{\prime}r_{k}^{d})=\exp(-ck).

Returning to (
7.20
), the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}}(\eta)={\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{in})}(\eta)\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{out})}(\eta)
, defined in (
7.17
), holds with probability tending to
1
as
k\to\infty
, again by concentration inequalities for Poisson random variables (see Lemma
C.1
for
x=2
).
Hence,

(7.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\big)-\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\neg({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\big)-o_{k}(1).

We recall from (
7.8
) that

{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}=\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\}\cap\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq kM_{\mathrm{out}}\}
. Translate the hyperrectangle
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
in (
7.7
) containing
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}
to the origin of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
(using
w_{\eta}(\ell))
in (
7.1
)):

(7.23)

{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime}:=\Lambda(0,kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho)\times[1,w_{\eta}(kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho))=\Lambda_{kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho,\eta},

and write
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out^{\prime}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out^{\prime}}}(0)
for the largest component and for the component containing
(0,w_{0})
in the subgraph of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
induced by vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{out}}
. As before, we ignore the conditioning
(0,w_{0})\in\Xi
in Definition
2.1
in our computations. By the translation invariance of the annealed measure and that the events
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\}
and
\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq M_{\mathrm{out}}k\}
are independent because they are induced subgraphs of the disjoint hyperrectangles
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
and
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
in (
7.7
),

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\big)=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\big)\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}^{\prime}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|\geq M_{\mathrm{out}}k\big)\geq\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)|\geq k\big)\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out}^{\prime}}(0)|\geq M_{\mathrm{out}}k\big).

The bound
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{\ell,\eta}(0)|\geq\rho\ell\big)\geq\rho
in (
7.3
) in Proposition
7.1
holds for all
\ell
sufficiently large.
In particular, since
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{in}}(0)={\mathcal{C}}_{k/\rho,\eta}(0)
by definition of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
in (
7.7
), and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{out^{\prime}}}(0)={\mathcal{C}}_{kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho,\eta}(0)
by definition of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}^{\prime}
in (
7.23
), we obtain for
k
sufficiently large

(7.24)

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\big)\geq\mathbb{P}^{{\scriptscriptstyle 0}}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{k/\rho,\eta}(0)|\geq k\big)\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{kM_{\mathrm{out}}/\rho,\eta}(0)|\geq M_{\mathrm{out}}k\big)\geq\rho^{2},

implying that
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)\geq\rho^{2}-o_{k}(1)>3\rho^{2}/4
in (
7.22
).
Since the event
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the subgraph
{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
, we take expectation over all possible realizations of the latter, and recalling the measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
from (
7.18
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\mid{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big[\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\eta)\big)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big[\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}_{\mathrm{io}}({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}})\big].

Apply now Lemma
7.2
on the right-hand side, and substitute the bound
3\rho^{2}/4
below (
7.24
) into (
7.22
) and then in turn into (
7.20
) and (
7.21
), to obtain for
k
sufficiently large

(7.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\big)

\displaystyle\geq(\rho^{2}/2)\exp\big(-Ar_{k}^{d\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(r_{k})\big)

\displaystyle\geq(\rho^{2}/2)\exp\big(-A^{\prime}k^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(k)\big).

We obtained the second row by substituting
r_{k}=(kM_{\mathrm{in}})^{1/d}
in (
7.6
) and setting
A^{\prime}:=AM_{\mathrm{in}}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}/2
that also compensates for the constants from the
\log
-correction term. Observe that
\max({\mathcal{Z}})<1
, so that the right-hand side in (
7.21
) is of smaller order than the right-hand side in (
7.19
).
By (
7.20
) this finishes the proof of (
7.4
). We turn to the proof of (
7.5
).

Lower bound on second-largest component.

We generalize an argument from
[
46
]
.
We have to bound
\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}\big)
from above for a suitably chosen
\varepsilon
in the definition of
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in (
7.2
).
To do so, we fix
\vartheta>0
to be specified later, and assume for simplicity that
n^{(1-\vartheta)/d}\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We then partition
\Lambda_{n}
into
m_{n}:=n^{1-\vartheta}
many subboxes
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\dots,\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(m_{n})}
, centered respectively at
x_{1},\dots,x_{m_{n}}
, each of volume
\tilde{n}:=n^{\vartheta}
.
By disjointness, the induced subgraphs
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\dots,{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(m_{n})}
in these boxes are independent realizations of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}
, translated to
x_{1},\dots,x_{m_{n}}
.
We write
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\dots,\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(m_{n})}
for the induced subgraphs, translated back to the origin;
\widetilde{\Xi}_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{in},i)}
for the vertex set in
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
that is below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}
and inside
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
after the translation, see (
7.11
); and write
\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{in},i)}
for the same vertex set before the translation.
For the translated subgraphs
\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})_{i\leq m_{n}}
, we define for
k=\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
the same events as in (
7.8
), (
7.14
), (
7.17
),

{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}_{\mathrm{good}}:={\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}),i}_{\mathrm{components}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}),i}_{\mathrm{isolation}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}),i}_{\mathrm{insmall}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}),i}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta),

where now in the definition of these events we replace
{\mathcal{C}}_{\Box}(0)
with the component containing the point of
\Xi
closest to the origin
0\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
for
\Box\in\{\mathrm{in},\widetilde{n}\}
.
We also assume that
\widetilde{n}=n^{\vartheta}
is sufficiently large compared to
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in (
7.2
) so that the spatial projection of the box
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
still fits within
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}
.
This can be ensured even if
k_{n,\varepsilon}=\Theta(n^{\varepsilon})
is maximal in (
7.2
) by choosing
\varepsilon<\vartheta
.
If
{\mathcal{A}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}_{\mathrm{good}}
holds for some
i\leq m_{n}
, then the induced graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
in subbox
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
contains a component
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{n}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}}
in
\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{in},i)}
(which we call a ‘candidate’ second-largest component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
) with size at least
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
that is not the largest component in its own box, and all vertices in
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
are below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}(x_{i})
, i.e.,
{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}
shifted to
x_{i}
.
If additionally for all
i
there is no edge between
\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{in},i)}
to a vertex outside
\Lambda_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
, then the component
{\mathcal{C}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
is not the giant and is larger than
k_{n,\varepsilon}
.
Taking complements we obtain that

\big\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}\}\subseteq\{\exists i\leq m_{n}:\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\mathrm{in},i)}\leftrightarrow\Xi_{n}\setminus\Xi_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}\}\cup\Bigg(\bigcap_{i\leq m_{n}}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}\big)\Bigg).

By translation invariance, a union bound, and the independence of
({\mathcal{G}}_{\tilde{n}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})_{i\leq m_{n}}
,

(7.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}|\leq\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}\big)

\displaystyle\leq m_{n}\mathbb{P}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\leftrightarrow\Xi_{n}\setminus\Xi_{\tilde{n}}\}\big)+\big(1-\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\big)^{m_{n}}=:T_{1}+T_{2}.

By the definitions in (
7.11
) and (
7.6
), each
u\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}
has
\|x_{u}\|\leq r_{k}
with
k=k_{n,\varepsilon}
, and mark
w_{u}\leq f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})
, (
f_{\star}=f_{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}
is from below (
7.13
)), hence

\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\subseteq\Xi_{(2r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{d}}[1,f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}}))\subseteq\Xi_{\tilde{n}}
, whenever
(2r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{d}=k_{n,\varepsilon}M_{\mathrm{in}}2^{d}<n^{\vartheta}
, which holds whenever
\varepsilon<\vartheta
by (
7.2
). Hence we can bound
T_{1}
as

T_{1}\leq m_{n}\mathbb{P}\big(\Xi_{(2r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{d}}[1,f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}}))\leftrightarrow\Xi_{n}\setminus\Xi_{\tilde{n}}\}\big).

We can directly apply Claim
6.6
on the right-hand side, i.e., setting there
N:=\tilde{n}=n^{\vartheta}
and
n:=(2r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{d}=k_{n,\varepsilon}M_{\mathrm{in}}2^{d}
(by (
7.6
)) and
\overline{w}:=f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})
.
The profile
f_{\star}=f_{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}
is defined below (
7.13
), using (
7.9
) with exponent
\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
and
r_{k}=(M_{\mathrm{in}}k)^{1/d}
in (
7.6
), and finally
k_{n,\epsilon}
from (
7.2
) we obtain

\overline{w}:=f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})=C_{\beta}^{-\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}d}r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}d}=C_{\beta}^{-\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}d}M_{\mathrm{in}}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}}

Condition (
6.26
) holds since
N=n^{\vartheta}\gg k_{n,\varepsilon}\gg\overline{w}
.
Then Claim
6.6
yields for some
C>0

\displaystyle\hskip-5.0ptT_{1}

\displaystyle\leq n^{1-\vartheta}C_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}f_{\star}(r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{c_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}}(2r_{\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}})^{d}n^{-\vartheta\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\}}\big(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\alpha=\tau-1}\log(n^{\vartheta})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\log(n)\cdot\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{1+\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}c_{\ref{claim:edge-long}}}\cdot n^{1-\vartheta\min\{\alpha,\tau-1\}}.

Since
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in (
7.2
) is at most
n^{\varepsilon}
, as long as
1-\vartheta\min(\alpha,\tau-1)<0
,
we can choose
\varepsilon>0
in (
7.2
) sufficiently small such that for any
\delta\in(0,\vartheta\min\{\alpha,\tau-1\}-1)
, for all
n
sufficiently large,

(7.27)

T_{1}\leq n^{-\delta}.

We turn to bound
T_{2}
in (
7.26
) using
(1-x)^{m_{n}}\leq\exp(-m_{n}x)
, where we apply (
7.25
) on
x=\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)})
to obtain a lower bound on the exponent

\displaystyle m_{n}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{good}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq(\rho^{2}/2)n^{1-\vartheta}\exp\big(-A^{\prime}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon})\big)

\displaystyle=(\rho^{2}/2)\exp\big((1-\vartheta)\log(n)-A^{\prime}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon})\big).

In order to show
T_{2}\leq n^{-\delta}
in (
7.26
), it is much stronger to show that with
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Z}})-1=\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}

(7.28)

\mbox{$\forall\varepsilon^{\prime}>0$, there exists $\varepsilon_{1}>0$ such that for all $\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{1}$: }A^{\prime}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon})<\varepsilon^{\prime}\log(n).

We recall the definition of
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in (
7.2
). While
k_{n,\varepsilon}
may be as large as
n^{\varepsilon}
, only when
\max({\mathcal{Z}})=0
, so effectively the expression in (
7.28
) is still small. We formally check:

Case 1.
\max({\mathcal{Z}})>0
.
We substitute
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in the first row of (
7.2
) to (
7.28
)

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon})=A^{\prime}\frac{\varepsilon\log(n)}{\log\log(n))^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}}\log^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}\Big(\big(\frac{\varepsilon\log(n)}{(\log\log(n))^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}}\big)^{1/\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\Big).

The last factor is at most
(\max{{\mathcal{Z}}})^{-\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}(\log\log(n))^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}
, and (
7.28
) follows whenever
\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}(\max{\mathcal{Z}})^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}/A^{\prime}.

Case 2.
\max({\mathcal{Z}})=0
.
We substitute
\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}
in the second row of (
7.2
) to (
7.28
)

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon}^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}(\underline{k}_{n,\varepsilon})=A^{\prime}\big(\log\big(\exp\big[(\varepsilon\log(n))^{1/\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}\big]\big)\big)^{\mathfrak{m}^{\prime}}=A^{\prime}\varepsilon\log(n),

and (
7.28
) again follows. Choose now any
\vartheta<1/\min\{\alpha,\tau-1\}
, and then combine (
7.27
)
with
T_{2}\leq n^{-\delta}
to bound (
7.26
). This finishes the proof of (
7.5
) and hence Proposition
7.1
subject to Lemma
7.2
.
∎

7.2.
Isolation

We aim to prove Lemma
7.2
.
We suppress the superscripts
(k)
of events, and leave it to the reader to verify that the results extend to the Palm-version
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}_{\mathrm{io}}
of
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
, which is defined in (
7.18
). We work towards bounding the event in (
7.19
) via a few lemmas/claims, also related to the back-of-the-envelope reasoning of Section
1.2
. Recall
f_{\gamma},M_{\gamma}
from (
7.9
), (
7.10
), the PPPs in (
7.11
), and
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\!=\!\tfrac{d-1}{d}
from (
1.9
).

Lemma 7.3

(Vertices above
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the conditions of Lemma
7.2
. For each
\gamma\in(0,1/(\sigma+1)]
,
there exists a constant
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-vertices-above}}>0
such that

(7.29)

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]\leq\begin{cases}C_{\ref{lemma:lower-vertices-above}}r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))},&\text{if }1-\gamma(\tau-1)>\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-vertices-above}}r_{k}^{d\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}}\log(r_{k}),&\text{if }1-\gamma(\tau-1)=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-vertices-above}}r_{k}^{d\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}},&\text{if }1-\gamma(\tau-1)<\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}.\end{cases}

Recall the multiplicity
\mathfrak{m}
of the maximum from (
2.5
).

Lemma 7.4

(Edges between vertices below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the conditions of Lemma
7.2
with
\alpha<\infty
. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{0,\alpha-(\tau-1),(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\}
.
For each
\gamma\in(0,1/(\sigma+1)]
there exists a constant
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}=C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}(\rho)>0
such that for any realization of
\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
that satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
in (
7.17
) for some
\eta>0
,

(7.30)

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)|\big]\leq\begin{cases}C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})-1}(r_{k}),&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})>\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}r_{k}^{d\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})}(r_{k}),&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}r_{k}^{d\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}},&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})<\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}.\end{cases}

Proof sketch of Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
.

We defer the lengthy integrals to the appendix on page
B

but we give some intuition.
The expectation
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}
in (
7.18
) is conditional on
\Xi\cap({\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}})
where
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
in (
7.7
) are hyperrectangles below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
for all
\eta
, since
w_{\eta}(\ell)
is poly-logarithmic in
\ell
in (
7.1
). Hence
\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}
is independent of the conditioning in
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}
, so

(7.31)

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]=\mathbb{E}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]=\mu_{\tau}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}\big),

with the intensity
\mu_{\tau}
in (
2.2
), and where we denote
{\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}
the points of
{\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}
above the
d
-dimensional surface
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
, see (
7.10
). Define the hyperrectangle
{\mathcal{R}}^{\uparrow}:=[-2r_{k},2r_{k}]^{d}\times[r_{k}^{\gamma},\infty)
and
\mathrm{A}_{\beta}:=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d},\|x\|\in[r_{k}-C_{\beta},r_{k}+C_{\beta}]\}\times[1,\infty)
, an annulus in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
times all mark-coordinates. Then by definition of
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
),
({\mathcal{R}}^{\uparrow}\cup\mathrm{A}_{\beta})\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}
, and
\mu_{\tau}({\mathcal{R}}^{\uparrow}\cup\mathrm{A}_{\beta})=\Theta(r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))}+r_{k}^{d-1})
.
Careful integration shows that the right-hand side of (
7.31
) is of the same order, unless
1-\gamma(\tau-1)=(d-1)/d=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
, when we get an extra
\log(r_{k})
factor.

For Lemma
7.4
, we use independence of
\Xi
in disjoint sets: inside and outside of
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
. When counting edges with at least one point in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
, we use that
\Xi
in these sets is regular, i.e.,
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}}
holds, and outside these sets we integrate using the intensity
\mu_{\tau}
in (
7.29
).
We explain now how the exponents of
r_{k}
in (
7.30
) arise:
the expected number of edges between vertices of constant mark within constant distance of
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
is
\Theta(r_{k}^{d-1})=\Theta(r_{k}^{d\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}})
, which yields lower bounds for all cases in (
7.30
). Let
0\leq\gamma_{1}\leq\gamma_{2}\leq\gamma
such that
\sigma\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}\leq\sigma+1
. Using
\mu_{\tau}
in (
2.2
), the expected number of pairs
(u,v)\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\times\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}
within distance
\Theta(r_{k})
from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, and marks
w_{u}=\Theta(k^{\gamma_{1}}),w_{v}=\Theta(k^{\gamma_{2}})
is

\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Pairs}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})]:=\Theta\big(r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma_{1}(\tau-1))}\cdot r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma_{2}(\tau-1))}\big)=\Theta\big(r_{k}^{d(2-(\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2})(\tau-1))}\big).

The typical Euclidean distance between such vertices is
\Theta(r_{k})
. Therefore, by the connection probability
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
), a pair of such vertices are connected with probability roughly
\Theta(r_{k}^{d\alpha(\sigma\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}-1)})
, and hence there are

(7.32)

\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Edges}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})]:=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Pairs}(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})]\cdot\Theta(r_{k}^{d\alpha(\sigma\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}-1)})=\Theta\big(r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma_{1}(\sigma\alpha-(\tau-1))+\gamma_{2}(\alpha-(\tau-1)))}\big).

such edges in expectation. The proof below on page
B
reveals that the expectation of
|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)|
can be bounded by maximizing (
7.32
) with respect to
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}
, i.e., edges between vertices of mark
\Theta(k^{\gamma_{1}^{\star}})
and
\Theta(k^{\gamma_{2}^{\star}})
, with the optimal pair
(\gamma_{1}^{\star},\gamma_{2}^{\star})
dominate the whole expectation.
Depending on
(\sigma,\alpha,\tau)
, the optimal
(\gamma_{1}^{\star},\gamma_{2}^{\star})
is either

(ll)

(0,0)
, pairs of low-mark vertices, which, when substituted in (
7.32
) yields
0\in{\mathcal{Y}}
in (
7.30
);

(lh)

(0,\gamma)
, pairs of one low-mark vertex and one high-mark vertex, which, when substituted in (
7.32
) yields
\alpha-(\tau-1)\in{\mathcal{Y}}
in (
7.30
);

(hh)

(\gamma,\gamma)
, pairs of high-mark vertices, which, when substituted in (
7.32
) yields
(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\in{\mathcal{Y}}
in (
7.30
);

or the convex combinations of these if the maximum in
{\mathcal{Y}}
is non-unique, leading to the poly-logarithmic correction terms in (
7.30
). These three options of
(\gamma_{1}^{\star},\gamma_{2}^{\star})
, combined with the
\Theta(r_{k}^{d-1})
many low-mark edges close to the boundary explain what we call the
dominant types of connectivity
in Section
1.2
. We remark that without the assumption that
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
, the optimal solution in (hh) would change to
(\gamma\wedge 1/(\sigma+1),\gamma\wedge 1/(\sigma+1))
.
∎

Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
hold for any
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
. Now we show that setting
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
from (
7.13
) minimizes the sum of (
7.29
) (increasing in
\gamma
) and (
7.30
) (decreasing in
\gamma
).

Claim 7.5

(The optimally-suppressed mark-profile)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the conditions of Lemma
7.2
. Let
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
. There exists a constant
C_{\ref{lemma:zeta-opt-other}}>0
such that when
\alpha<\infty

(7.33)

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]\leq C_{\ref{lemma:zeta-opt-other}}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)|\big]\leq C_{\ref{lemma:zeta-opt-other}}C_{\ref{lemma:lower-edges-below}}r_{k}^{d\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(r_{k}),

and when
\alpha=\infty

(7.34)

\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]\leq C_{\ref{lemma:zeta-opt-other}}r_{k}^{d\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(r_{k}).

We postpone the proof, based on elementary rearrangements of the formulas of
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},
and
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
, to the appendix on page
C
.
We proceed with a claim towards bounding the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
in (
7.12
), its proof explaining the restriction
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
in
\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
in (
7.13
).
We recall
\mathrm{p}(u,v)
defined in (
2.3
).

Claim 7.6

(Cross-edge probability bounds)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the conditions of Lemma
7.2
, and assume
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
. For any
(u,v)\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\times\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}

(7.35)

\mathrm{p}(u,v)\leq\begin{cases}2^{-\alpha},&\text{if }\alpha<\infty,\\
0,&\text{if }\alpha=\infty.\end{cases}

Proof.

We show that whenever
u,v
are below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
and on different sides of
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, then
\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u},w_{v})/

\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}\leq 1/2
. By definition of
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
), this directly implies (
7.35
).
To see this bound, for
\sigma\geq 0
the connection probability
\mathrm{p}
is increasing in the marks.
Therefore, without loss of generality we will assume that
u\in{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
and
v\notin{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
fall exactly on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
, and that
k
is large enough that
r_{k}\geq C_{\beta}
. Since
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
) switches formula at
r_{k}
outside
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
, we distinguish two cases.

Case 1. Assume
|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|\leq r_{k}
.

Since
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
) and the explanation below (
7.12
) both
u,v
are at least
C_{\beta}
distance from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
.
Thus, for any
(u,v)
, there exist
t\geq 2C_{\beta}
,
\nu\in(0,1)
such that
\|x_{u}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|=r_{k}-\|x_{u}\|=(1-\nu)t
, and
\|x_{v}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|=\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}=\nu t
. By the triangle inequality
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\geq t
.
Hence,
w_{v}=C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d}(\nu t)^{\gamma d},w_{u}=C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d}((1-\nu)t)^{\gamma d}
by assuming
u,v
being on
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
and
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
). Using
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
in (
1.10
), and
\nu\in(0,1)

\displaystyle\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u},w_{v})}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d(\sigma+1)}\frac{\max\{(1-\nu)t,\nu t\}^{\gamma d}\min\{(1-\nu)t,\nu t\}^{\sigma\gamma d}}{t^{d}}\leq\beta C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d(\sigma+1)}t^{d(\gamma(\sigma+1)-1)}.

The right-hand side is non-increasing in
t

whenever

\gamma\leq\frac{1}{\sigma+1}
. Since
t\geq 2C_{\beta}\geq C_{\beta}
, and
C_{\beta}=(2\beta)^{1/d}

\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u},w_{v})}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}}\leq\beta C_{\beta}^{-d\gamma(\sigma+1)}C_{\beta}^{d(\gamma(\sigma+1)-1)}=\beta C_{\beta}^{-d}=1/2.

Case 2. Assume
|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|>r_{k}
.
In this case
\|x_{v}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|\geq\|x_{u}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|
, and
\|x_{v}-x_{u}\|\geq\|x_{v}-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|=|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|
.
Since
f_{\gamma}(z)
is increasing,
w_{v}=f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)\geq f_{\gamma}(r_{k})\geq f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{u}\|-r_{k}|)=w_{u}
. We obtain by definition of
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
in (
1.10
) and (
7.9
)

\displaystyle\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u},w_{v})}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}}\!\leq\!\beta\frac{f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|)f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{u}\|-r_{k}|)^{\sigma}}{|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|^{d}}

\displaystyle\!\leq\!\beta C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d(\sigma+1)}|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|^{d}r_{k}^{-d(1-\gamma)}r_{k}^{\sigma\gamma d}|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|^{-d}

\displaystyle=\beta C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d(\sigma+1)}r_{k}^{-d(1-\gamma(\sigma+1))}\leq 1/2.

where to obtain the second inequality we used that
f_{\gamma}(|\|x_{u}\|-r_{k}|)^{\sigma}\leq r_{k}^{\sigma\gamma d}
, and to obtain the second row the power of
|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|
canceled. The bound
1/2
follows because
r_{k}\geq C_{\beta}
,
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
and
C_{\beta}=(2\beta)^{1/d}
.
∎

We are ready to prove Lemma
7.2
.

Proof of Lemma
7.2
.

We will obtain a lower bound on
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\{\Xi\!\leq\!{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)
for
arbitrary

\gamma\in(0,1/(\sigma+1)]
.
Then we maximize this bound by setting
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
, yielding the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{isolation}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
in (
7.14
) and the bound in Lemma
7.2
.
The events
\{\Xi\!\leq\!{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
(defined in (
7.12
)) are independent of each other under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
in (
7.18
), since having no points above
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
is independent of the conditioning in
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
(since each point in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}\cup{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
is below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
if
k
is sufficiently large), and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
only depends on points of
\Xi
below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
. Hence

(7.36)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\big)\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big).

We analyze the two probabilities separately. For the first term, by the above independence,

(7.37)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\big)=\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|=0\big)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(-\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]\big),

for which we will use Lemma
7.3
shortly. We now turn to the second factor in (
7.36
).
By definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
in (
7.12
), and using the conditional independence of edges,

(7.38)

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Bigg[\prod_{u\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},v\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}}(1-\mathrm{p}(u,v))\Bigg].

We distinguish now the two cases
\alpha=\infty
and
\alpha<\infty
.

Case
\alpha=\infty
. By Claim
7.6
, since
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
by assumption,
\mathrm{p}(u,v)=0
for each factor, hence

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)=1
. Since the right-hand side of (
7.37
) is increasing in
\gamma
, we set
\gamma=1/(\sigma+1)=\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
when
\alpha=\infty
by (
7.13
).
Combining then (
7.36
) with (
7.37
) and Claim
7.5
, it follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\star}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}})\big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\big(-C_{\ref{lemma:lower-vertices-above}}r_{k}^{d\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(r_{k})\big),

finishing the proof of the lemma for
\alpha=\infty
.

Case
\alpha<\infty
. By Claim
7.6
, for all
(u,v)\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\times\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}
it holds that

1-\mathrm{p}(u,v)\geq 1-2^{-\alpha}
since
\gamma\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
by assumption. Hence, there exists a constant
c>0
such that for all such
(u,v)
we have
