1-\mathrm{p}(u,v)\geq\exp(-c\cdot\mathrm{p}(u,v))
.
Using this in (
7.38
) and that
s\mapsto\exp(-s)
is a convex function, Jensen’s inequality gives a lower bound in terms of the expected number of edges between vertices below
{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Bigg[\exp\Bigg(-c\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\\
v\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{p}(u,v)\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Bigg(-c\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\\
v\in\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\end{subarray}}\mathrm{p}(u,v)\Bigg]\Bigg)

(7.39)

\displaystyle=\exp\big(-c\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)|\big]\big).

Together with (
7.36
) and (
7.37
), we obtain that

(7.40)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big(\{\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{noedge}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}(\gamma)\big)

\displaystyle\geq\exp\Big(-\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}|\big]+c\,\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\big[|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)|\big]\big)\Big).

By Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
, the first expectation is decreasing, while the second one is increasing in
\gamma
on the right-hand side. By (
7.33
) in Lemma
7.5
, when we set
\gamma=\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
in (
7.13
), we obtain that (
7.40
) turns into (
7.19
),
finishing the proof of Lemma
7.2
when
{\mathcal{V}}=\Xi
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.2
for i-KSRGs on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
.

We explain how to adjust the proof to i-KSRGs on
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
using the assumption
\min\{p,p\beta^{\alpha}\}<1
in Lemma
7.2
.
Since the vertex set
{\mathcal{V}}={\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
, the event
\Xi\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}
as defined in (
7.10
) would never hold, since
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
does have points within distance
C_{\beta}
from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
(see
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
) and the reasoning below (
7.12
)). Hence, we must adjust the definition of
f_{\gamma}
within distance
C_{\beta}
of
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
to be any constant
c>1
.
Then the upper bound
2^{-\alpha}
on
\mathrm{p}(u,v)
in Claim
7.6
can be replaced by
p<1

(the edge-retention probability for vertices at distance 1 in (
2.3
)), that only affects constant prefactors in (
7.39
) and (
7.40
).

The proofs of the preliminary Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
remain valid by replacing concentration bounds for Poisson random variables by concentration bounds for Binomial random variables, and replacing integrals over
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
by summations over
{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
. The proof of Claim
7.5
remains verbatim valid.
∎

Proofs of Theorems
2.2
,
2.4
, and Corollary
2.3
.

Proposition
7.1
yields the lower bounds for both theorems, since its condition (
7.3
) on having a large enough component on restricted marks occurs with positive probability by Proposition
5.14
, under the assumption
\tau<2+\sigma
. The assumption
\tau>2
is necessary to have a locally finite graph.
Propositions
5.1
and
6.1
imply the upper bounds (i.e., part (ii)) in Theorems
2.4
,
2.2
, and Corollary
2.3
, respectively. The conditions in Proposition
6.1
are satisfied by Propositions
5.1
,
5.12
and Corollary
5.11
that all hold for the Palm-version
\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle x}
of
\mathbb{P}
as well. The condition
\tau<2+\sigma
is needed for Claim
5.5
,
which yields that a constant proportion of high-mark vertices, i.e., vertices having mark
\Omega(k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}})
, is connected by an edge to another high-mark vertex.
This allows to construct a backbone of high-mark vertices (Lemma
5.2
), and to merge components of size at least
k
with the backbone via a high-mark vertex in (
5.42
).
The additional condition
\sigma\leq\tau-1
in Theorems
2.2
and
2.4
is needed in the cover-expansion step (Lemma
5.9
) in the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

It remains to prove Theorem
2.5
, whose proof is based on Lemma
7.2
.

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

For
\rho\geq 1
the statement is trivial.
There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
\rho\in(0,1)
and
n\geq 1
a box of volume
n
is contained in the union of
\lceil C/\rho\rceil
(partially overlapping) balls of volume
n\rho/2
. Fix
\rho\in(0,1)
, and write
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
for the vertices in the
i
-th ball of such a cover of balls of volume
n\rho/2
.
Recall that
|{\mathcal{E}}(A,B)|
denotes the number of edges between the sets
A,B
. Then

(7.41)

\{|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<\rho n\}\supseteq\bigcap_{i\leq\lceil C/\rho\rceil}\{|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},\Xi\setminus\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})|=0\}\cap\{|\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}|<\rho n\}

Indeed, on the event on the right-hand side, each connected component of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n}
is fully contained in some ball (or the intersection of some balls) with at most
\rho n
vertices.
We apply an FKG inequality to bound the probability of intersection from below.

We give a (natural) definition of increasing events, using the collection
\Psi
from Definition
5.3
that encodes the presence of edges using a set of uniform random variables
\Psi_{\Xi}=\{\varphi_{u,v}:u,v\in\Xi\}
. We say that a function
f(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})
defined on the marked vertex set
\Xi
and edge-variable set
\Psi_{\Xi}
is
increasing
if it is non-decreasing in
\Xi
with respect to set inclusion (formally, if
\Xi^{\prime}\supseteq\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi^{\prime}}\supseteq\Psi_{\Xi}
, then
f(\Xi^{\prime},\Psi_{\Xi^{\prime}})\geq f(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})
holds),
as well as coordinate-wise non-increasing with respect to the edge variables (formally, if
\Psi^{\prime}_{\Xi}
satisfies
\varphi^{\prime}_{u,v}\leq\varphi_{u,v}
for all
u,v\in{\binom{\Xi}{2}}
, then
f(\Xi,\Psi^{\prime}_{\Xi})\geq f(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})
holds). Intuitively this means that more vertices and edges increase the value of
f
.
Similarly to
[
18
]
, we obtain that for two increasing functions
f_{1}
,
f_{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f_{1}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\cdot f_{2}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})]=\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[f_{1}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\cdot f_{2}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\mid\Xi]\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbb{E}[f_{1}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\mid\Xi]\cdot\mathbb{E}[f_{2}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\mid\Xi]\big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}[f_{1}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})\big]\cdot\mathbb{E}[f_{2}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})],

by applying FKG to the random graph conditioned to have
\Xi
as its vertex set for the first inequality, and then FKG for point processes for the second inequality
[
52
, Theorem 20.4]
.
We say that an event
{\mathcal{A}}
is decreasing iff the function
-\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{A}}
is increasing. It follows that for decreasing events
{\mathcal{A}},{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}

(7.42)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}\cap{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\big)=\mathbb{E}[(-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi}))\cdot(-\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi}))]\geq\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})]\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}}(\Xi,\Psi_{\Xi})]=\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}\big)\cdot\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}\big).

Observe that the events on the right-hand side in (
7.41
) are all decreasing (adding vertices/edges make the events less likely to occur) so that (
7.42
) applies. Hence,

(7.43)

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}|<\rho n\big)\geq\prod_{i\leq\lceil C/\rho\rceil}\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},\Xi\setminus\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})|=0\big)\cdot\mathbb{P}\big(|\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}|<\rho n\big).

Since each ball has volume
n\rho/2
, the event
\{|\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}|<\rho n\}
holds with probability at least
1/2
by concentration inequalities for Poisson random variables (Lemma
C.1
for
x=2
). To bound
\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},\Xi\setminus\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})|=0\big)
, we consider the optimally-suppressed mark profile translated to the center of the
i
-th ball, with
kM_{\mathrm{in}}
replaced by
n\rho/2
. We restrict
\Xi
to be below the mark profile, and to have no edges between
\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
and
\Xi\setminus\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}
.
We apply Lemma
7.2
, integrate over all realisations of
{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{in}},{\mathcal{G}}_{\mathrm{out}}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}
, and use that
the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}
in the conditioning in
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{io}}
in (
7.18
) holds with high probability by Poisson concentration (Lemma
C.1
for
x=2
), see the argument below (
7.21
).
We obtain that for all
i\leq\lceil C/\rho\rceil
,

\mathbb{P}\big(|{\mathcal{E}}(\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)},\Xi\setminus\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)})|=0\big)\cdot\mathbb{P}\big(|\Xi^{\scriptscriptstyle(i)}|<\rho n\big)\geq\exp\big(-\Theta(n^{\max({\mathcal{Z}})}\log^{\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1}(n))\big)/2,

which yields the desired statement in (
2.11
) when taking the product over
\lceil C/\rho\rceil
balls in (
7.43
).
∎

Appendix A
Proofs based on backbone construction

We present the proofs of the propositions at the end of Section
5
.

Proof of Proposition
5.12
.

We will first derive a bound on
\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)
for arbitrary
\overline{w}\geq 1
.
We make use of the backbone construction from Section
5
: we will show that vertices with mark at least
\overline{w}
are likely to connect to the backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, which will be a subset of the giant component. Observe that the event in (
5.44
) allows us to choose the size of the boxes when we build the backbone, i.e., the value of
k
is not yet defined with respect to
\overline{w}
. We define
k=k(\overline{w})
implicitly by
\overline{w}=:A_{1}k^{1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}
, where
A_{1}
is a large enough constant to be determined later. We aim to show that for some
A_{2}>0
,

(A.1)

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})\big):=\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[A_{1}k^{1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}},\infty):v\notin{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\big)\leq n\exp\big(-A_{2}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big).

If this bound holds, then substituting
\overline{w}=\overline{w}_{n}=(M_{w}\log(n))^{(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
yields

k(\overline{w}_{n})=A_{1}^{-1/(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})}\overline{w}_{n}^{1/(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})}=A_{1}^{-1/(1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}})}(M_{w}\log n)^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}},

which, when substituted back to (
A.1
) yields (
5.44
) if
M_{w}
is chosen sufficiently large.

Recall
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
from (
5.8
).
Distinguishing two cases depending on whether
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
holds for
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
or not (with
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k)
in (
5.7
)), by Lemma
5.2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)\big]

(A.2)

\displaystyle\leq 3n\exp(-c_{\ref{lemma:upper-hh-bb}}s_{k})+\mathbb{E}\big[\mathbbm{1}_{\{{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}}\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)\big].

On the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, there is a backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
. This backbone is either not part of the giant component, or if it is, then a vertex with mark at least
\overline{w}
outside the giant has
no connection
to any of the vertices in the backbone. Hence, conditionally on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
,

\displaystyle\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})\subseteq\{{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\nsubseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}\cup\{\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty):v\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}},{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\subseteq{\mathcal{C}}_{n}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)}\}.

By a union bound and Corollary
5.10
, this implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{mark-giant}}(n,\overline{w})

\displaystyle\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)

(A.3)

\displaystyle\leq(n/c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}})\exp\big(-c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}}k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty):v\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big).

Recall that
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
is the graph spanned on vertices with mark in
[1,2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, see Definition
5.6
.
With
C_{1}
from (
5.5
)–(
5.6
), we may assume
A_{1}\geq 2C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}
. Since
w_{\mathrm{hh}}=C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}
defined in (
5.7
), and since
1-(1+\sigma)\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}\geq 0
(see (
5.14
)), this implies that

\overline{w}=A_{1}k^{1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}\geq 2w_{\mathrm{hh}}=2C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}.

Hence, vertices of mark at least
\overline{w}
are part of
\Xi_{n}[2w_{\mathrm{hh}},\infty)
and are not revealed in
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
.
Conditioning on the number of vertices
|\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)|
, the location of each vertex is independent and uniform in
\Lambda_{n}
. Taking a union bound over these vertices in
\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)
, yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)

\displaystyle:u\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[|\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)|]\cdot\mathbb{P}\big(v\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}},v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)\big)

(A.4)

\displaystyle\leq n\,\mathbb{P}\big(v\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}},v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)\big).

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}(v)
be as in (
5.3
).
Conditionally on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
,
{\mathcal{Q}}(v)
contains at least
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
vertices in
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
with mark in
[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, where
w_{\mathrm{hh}}
is defined in (
5.7
), yielding the set of vertices
{\mathcal{S}}(v)
in (
5.22
). We use the distance bound in (
5.4
),
and
\mathrm{p}
,
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
defined in (
2.3
), and (
1.10
), respectively, and the value
\overline{w}
in (
A
), to obtain that for any
v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty)
and
u\in{\mathcal{S}}(v)
, when
\alpha<\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{p}(u,v)

\displaystyle\geq p\min\big\{1,\big(\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{\mathrm{hh}},A_{1}k^{1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}})(2\sqrt{d})^{-d}k^{-1}\big)^{\alpha}\big\}

\displaystyle=p\min\big\{1,\big(\beta C_{1}^{-\sigma/(\tau-1)}k^{\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}A_{1}k^{1-\sigma\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}(2\sqrt{d})^{-d}k^{-1}\big)^{\alpha}\big\}=p,

whenever
A_{1}\geq(2\sqrt{d})^{d}C_{1}^{\sigma/(\tau-1)}/\beta
,
since the exponent of
k
in the second term of the minimum is
0
.
The same bound holds when
\alpha=\infty
.
Since
v
connects by an edge to each of the
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}})
many backbone vertices in
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
with probability at least
p
, conditionally independently of each other, we bound (
A.4
) by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi_{n}[\overline{w},\infty):v\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\mid{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq n(1-p)^{s_{k}}.

Since
s_{k}=\Theta(k^{\zeta_{\mathrm{h}h}})
in (
5.7
), combining this with (
A.2
) and (
A.3
) yields (
A.1
) for
A_{2}
sufficiently small. As argued below (
A.1
), this yields (
5.44
).
∎

A.1.
Construction of a linear-sized component

This section presents the proof of the first statement of Proposition
5.14
. At the end of the section, we comment how the proof can be adjusted to obtain the second statement considering the infinite model. Throughout the proof, we will consider the Palm version of
\mathbb{P}
, conditioning
\Xi
to contain a vertex at location 0. We will leave it out in the notation.
We will show using a second-moment method that linearly many vertices connect to the backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k)
for a properly chosen
k
. First we introduce some notation.
Recall
C_{1}
from (
5.5
)-(
5.6
). We implicitly define
k=k_{n}
as the solution of the equation

(A.5)

(C_{1}/16)k_{n}^{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}:=(2/c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}})\log(n),

yielding
m=(2/c_{\ref{prop:2nd-upper-bound-hh}})^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}16/C_{1}
in the statement of Proposition
5.14
, and the mark-truncation value in the definition of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}
at
2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})=2C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k_{n}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}
from (
5.7
), with
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})=(M_{w}\log(n))^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
for some constant
M_{w}
.
We reveal the realization of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}={\mathcal{G}}_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
(defined above (
5.5
)), conditioned to satisfy the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})
in (
5.8
).
Recalling the intensity measure of the Poisson vertex set
\mu_{\tau}
from (
2.2
), for any constant
m_{w}\geq 1
we define the event

(A.6)

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}:=\big\{|\Xi_{\mathrm{n}}[m_{w},2m_{w})|\big/\mu_{\tau}\big(\Lambda_{n}\times[m_{w},2m_{w})\big)\,\in[1/2,2]\big\},

that is, that the PPP in
\Lambda_{n}
is regular in the sense that the number of constant-mark vertices is roughly as expected. Writing
w_{0}
for the mark of
0
, we define the conditional probability measure

(A.7)

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(\,\cdot\,):=\mathbb{P}\big(\,\cdot\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n}),{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1},w_{0}\in[m_{w},2m_{w})\big),

with corresponding expectation
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
. We state a lemma that implies Proposition
5.14
.

Lemma A.1

(Constructing a component)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the same conditions as Proposition
5.14
.
Take
k_{n}
as in (
A.5
). For any
m_{w}\geq 1
, for all sufficiently large
n
,

(A.8)

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n}),w_{0}\in[m_{w},2m_{w})\big)\geq m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}/2.

Moreover, there exist constants
m_{w}\geq 1,\rho>0
, such that for all sufficiently large
n

(A.9)

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)|\geq\rho_{\ref{lemma:construction}}n\big)\geq\rho_{\ref{lemma:construction}}.

To prove the lemma (in particular the second statement), we need to define auxiliary notation and an auxiliary claim: we define for
u:=(x_{u},w_{u})\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})
the event

(A.10)

\{u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}:=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mbox{$\exists$ a path in ${\mathcal{G}}_{n,2}$ from $u$ to some vertex in ${\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})$, with all}\\
&\mbox{vertices (except $u$) having mark in ${\mathbb{R}}^{+}\setminus[m_{w},2m_{w})$}\end{aligned}\right\}.

Recall
\Lambda(0,s)
from (
2.1
) the box centered at
0
of volume
s
, and that
u=(x_{u},w_{u})
denotes the location and mark of a vertex
u\in\Xi
. The next claim states that if
u
and
0
are both vertices with mark in
[m_{w},2m_{w})
, falling into different subboxes
{\mathcal{Q}}(0)\neq{\mathcal{Q}}(u)
((
5.3
)) with respect to the boxing defined by
k_{n}
above (
5.3
), then the event that both
0
and
u
connect to the backbone
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
happens with constant probability.

Claim A.2

(Paths to the backbone)
.

Consider a supercritical i-KSRG under the conditions of Proposition
5.14
. There exist positive constants
m_{w},q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}},C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}>0
such that for all
u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})
with
x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})
, and
n
sufficiently large

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(\{0\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})\}\cap\{u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})\}\mid u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}),x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})\big)\geq q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}.

Proof.

We will build what we call "mark-increasing paths". Recall
k_{n}=(m\log(n))^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
in Prop.
5.14
and that
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})=(M_{w}\log(n))^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}
computed below (
A.5
). Define

(A.11)

j_{\ast}:=\max\{j:2^{j+1}m_{w}<w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})\},

and define for
0\leq j\leq j_{\ast}
and
x\in\Lambda_{n}
the following boxes and disjoint mark intervals

(A.12)

\displaystyle Q_{j}(x):=\Lambda\big(x,\beta 2^{-\sigma}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1}\big)\cap\Lambda_{n},\qquad I_{j}:=[2^{j}m_{w},2^{j+1}m_{w}),

and write
Q_{j_{\ast}+1}:={\mathcal{Q}}(x)
, i.e., the volume-
k_{n}
subbox containing
x
in the partitioning of
\Lambda_{n}
for the backbone construction given in (
5.3
), and define also
I_{j_{\ast}+1}:=[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
.
Even with the truncation by
\Lambda_{n}
in (
A.12
), the volume bound

(A.13)

\beta 2^{-\sigma-d}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1}\leq\mathrm{Vol}(Q_{j}(x))\leq\beta 2^{-\sigma}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1}

holds for all
x\in\Lambda_{n}
and
j\leq j_{\ast}
similarly to (
4.4
).
By (
2.2
), the number of vertices of
\Xi
in
Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j}
has Poisson distribution with mean

(A.14)

\mu_{\tau}\big(Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j}\big)\geq 2^{-1}\beta(2^{j}m_{w})^{-(\tau-1)}2^{-\sigma-d}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1},

where we used that
1-2^{-(\tau-1)}\geq 2^{-1}
for
\tau>2
.
Moreover, (
A.11
) implies
2^{j}m_{w}\leq w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})
for all
j\leq j_{\star}
and substituting this in (
A.12
) with
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})=C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k_{n}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}
from (
5.7
) yields

\displaystyle Q_{j}(x)

\displaystyle\subseteq\Lambda\big(x,\beta 2^{-\sigma}d^{-d/2}(w_{\mathrm{hh}}(n))^{\sigma+1}\big)=\Lambda\big(x,\beta 2^{-\sigma}d^{-d/2}C_{1}^{-(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)}k_{n}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\sigma+1)}\big)

\displaystyle\subseteq\Lambda\big(x,\beta 2^{-\sigma}d^{-d/2}C_{1}^{-(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)}k_{n}\big)=:Q^{\star}(x),

where the last inclusion follows from
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
by (
5.14
). Let
\mathrm{diam}(Q^{\star}(x))=:Ck_{n}^{1/d}
denote the diameter of
Q^{\star}(x)
, and let
Q^{\diamond}:=\Lambda(0,2^{1/d}Ck_{n})
denote the box centered at
0
, such that
\mathrm{diam}(Q^{\diamond})=2\mathrm{diam}(Q^{\star})
. For any
x_{u}\notin{\mathcal{Q}}^{\diamond}
, and all pairs
j,j^{\prime}\leq j_{\ast}
, it holds that

(A.15)

Q_{j}(x_{u})\cap Q_{j^{\prime}}(0)=\emptyset,

and thus the PPPs restricted to
Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j}
are independent of each other and of
Q_{j^{\prime}}(0)\times I_{j^{\prime}}
for all
j,j^{\prime}\leq j_{\ast}
. On the conditional measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, defined in (
A.7
), we fixed (revealed) the realization of
\Xi_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
. Edges among
\Xi_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
and
\Xi\cap(Q_{j_{*}}(x_{u})\times I_{j_{*}})
are thus also present conditionally independently.
We define for
u=(x_{u},w_{u})=:u_{0}
the event of having a "mark-increasing path" (a subevent of
\{u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}
defined in (
A.10
)):

(A.16)

\{u\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}:=\{\exists(u_{1},\dots,u_{j_{\ast}+1}),u_{j_{*}+1}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}:\forall j\in[j_{\ast}+1]:u_{j}\in Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j},u_{j-1}\leftrightarrow u_{j}\}.

on which there is a path from
u
to the backbone, where the
j
th vertex on the path is in
Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j}
(that are disjoint across
j
). The mark of
u_{j_{*}+1}
is in the right range by definition
I_{j_{\ast}+1}
of below (
A.12
).
By this disjointness and (
A.15
), the events
\{0\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}
and
\{u\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}
are independent conditionally on
\Xi_{n}[w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}),2w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n}))
.

To bound
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(u\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}})
from below, we greedily ‘construct’ a path from
u=u_{0}
to the backbone. By assumption
w_{u}\in[m_{w},2m_{w})
hence
u_{0}\in Q_{0}(x_{u})\times I_{0}
. We first bound the probability that
u_{0}
connects by an edge to a vertex
u_{1}\in Q_{1}(x_{u})\times I_{1}
. Then, if there is such a connection, we choose
u_{1}
to be an arbitrary vertex connected to
u_{0}
, and give a uniform lower bound (over the possible
u_{1}
) on the probability that it connects by an edge to a vertex
u_{2}\in Q_{2}(x_{u})\times I_{2}
. We continue this process until we reach
u_{j_{\ast}}
that has mark just smaller than the minimal mark of vertices in the backbone, by definition of
j_{\ast}
in (
7.15
).
Then we find a connection from
u_{j_{\ast}}
to the backbone.
We now bound the probability that two vertices
u_{j-1}
and
u_{j}
are connected by an edge.

By construction,
w_{u_{j-1}}\geq 2^{j-1}m_{w}
,
w_{u_{j}}\geq 2^{j}m_{w}
. Hence,
with the kernel
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
from (
1.10
), and volume bound (
A.13
), we obtain

\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u_{j-1}},w_{u_{j}})=\beta w_{u_{j-1}}^{\sigma}w_{u_{j}}\geq\beta m_{w}^{\sigma+1}2^{(\sigma+1)j-\sigma}\geq\beta 2^{-\sigma}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1}d^{d/2}\mathrm{Vol}(Q_{j}(x_{u})).

Further, their distance
\|x_{u_{j-1}}-x_{u_{j}}\|^{d}\leq d^{d/2}\mathrm{Vol}(Q_{j}(x_{u}))\leq\beta 2^{-\sigma}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+1}
by (
A.12
), hence

\mathrm{p}(u_{j-1},u_{j})\geq p\min\Big\{1,\frac{(\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u_{j}},w_{u_{j+1}}))^{\alpha}}{\|x_{u_{j}}-x_{u_{j+1}}\|^{\alpha d}}\Big\}=p.

The same computation (without
\alpha
in the exponent) is valid for
\alpha=\infty
. Having already chosen
u_{j-1}
on the path, each
v\in\Xi_{n}\cap(Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j})
connects independently by an edge to
u_{j-1}
with probability
p
. The conditioning in the measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
A.7
) only affects the number of vertices with mark in
[m_{w},2m_{w})
and in
[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
. Due to independence of the number of points of PPPs in disjoint sets, for
j\leq j_{\ast}
, the number of candidate vertices for the role of
u_{j}
is thus stochastically dominated from below by a
\mathrm{Poi}\big(p\mu_{\tau}(Q_{j}(x_{u})\times I_{j})\big)
random variable. The mean is at least
p\beta 2^{-\sigma-d-1}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+2-\tau}
by (
2.2
) and (
A.14
). For
j=j_{\ast}+1
we use that the vertices in the backbone in
Q_{j_{\ast}+1}\times I_{j_{\ast}+1}
are exactly those in
{\mathcal{Q}}(u)\times[w_{\mathrm{hh}},2w_{\mathrm{hh}})
, that we denoted by
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
in (
5.22
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(\neg\{u\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(u_{j_{\ast}}\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big)+\sum_{j=1}^{j_{\ast}}\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(p\beta 2^{-\sigma-d-1}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+2-\tau})=0\big)

(A.17)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(u_{j_{\ast}}\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big)+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\exp\big(-p\beta 2^{-\sigma-d-1}d^{-d/2}(2^{j}m_{w})^{\sigma+2-\tau}\big).

Since
\tau<2+\sigma
by assumption, the sum is finite and decreasing in
m_{w}
.
By definition of
j_{\ast}
and
I_{j_{\ast}}
in (
A.11
),
w_{u_{j_{\ast}}}\geq 2^{j_{\ast}}m_{w}\geq w_{\mathrm{hh}}/4
.
We recall that
{\mathcal{Q}}(x_{u})
is the subbox of volume
k_{n}
in the partitioning for the backbone containing
x_{u}
((
5.3
)), that contains at least
s_{k_{n}}
backbone vertices of mark at least
w_{\mathrm{hh}}(k_{n})
, both defined in (
5.7
). When
\alpha<\infty
, we follow the computations in (
5.17
) (that are also valid under the conditional measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
from (
A.7
), ensuring that
{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n})
exists and
|{\mathcal{S}}(u)|\geq s_{k_{n}}
by
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
5.8
)), and use that
u_{j_{\ast}}
is at distance at most
2\sqrt{d}k_{n}^{1/d}
from any vertex in
{\mathcal{S}}(u)
, to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(u_{j_{\ast}}\not\leftrightarrow{\mathcal{S}}(u)\big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\big(1-p\min\{1,\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}2^{-(2\sigma+d)\alpha}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}k_{n}^{-\alpha}\}\big)^{s_{k_{n}}}

(A.18)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\exp\big(p\min\{s_{k_{n}},\beta^{\alpha}d^{-\alpha d/2}2^{-(2\sigma+d)\alpha}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}k_{n}^{-\alpha}s_{k_{n}}\}\big).

Using that
s_{k_{n}}=k_{n}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{-(\tau-1)}/16
,
w_{\mathrm{hh}}=C_{1}^{-1/(\tau-1)}k_{n}^{\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}}
, and
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}=(\alpha-1)/((\sigma+1)\alpha-(\tau-1))
, we have

w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha}k_{n}^{-\alpha}s_{k_{n}}\geq 2^{-4}w_{\mathrm{hh}}^{(1+\sigma)\alpha-(\tau-1)}k_{n}^{1-\alpha}=2^{-4}C_{1}^{1-(1+\sigma)\alpha/(\tau-1)}.

Using this bound on the right-hand side in (
A.18
), this yields combined with (
A.17
) that there exists a constant
q>0
such that if
m_{w}
is sufficiently large

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(u\leadsto{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)\geq q

establishing Claim
A.2
when
\alpha<\infty
, by the reasoning about independence below (
A.16
).

When
\alpha=\infty
, the choice of
C_{1}
in (
5.6
) ensures that for any vertex
u_{\mathrm{bb}}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap{\mathcal{Q}}(u)
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{p}(u_{j_{\ast}},u_{\mathrm{bb}})\geq p\mathbbm{1}\Big\{\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{\mathrm{hh}}/4,w_{\mathrm{hh}})}{2^{d}d^{d/2}k_{n}}\geq 1\Big\}=p,

establishing Lemma
A.2
for
\alpha=\infty
when combined with (
A.17
) for
m_{w}
sufficiently large.
∎

We are now ready to prove Lemma
A.1
.

Proof of Lemma
A.1
.

We first show (
A.8
). By a union bound, concentration inequalities for Poisson random variables (Lemma
C.1
for
x\in\{1/2,2\}
) and
F_{W}(\mathrm{d}w_{0})=(\tau-1)w_{0}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w
in Definition
2.1
, it follows that
\mathbb{P}(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime})=\exp(-\Theta(nm_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}))=o(1)
, and thus

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\big(\neg

\displaystyle\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap\{w_{0}\in[m_{w},2m_{w})\}\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)+\mathbb{P}\big(w_{0}\notin[m_{w},2m_{w})\big)+o(1)\leq\mathbb{P}\big(\neg{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big)+1-(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}+o(1).

By the choice of
k=k_{n}
in (
A.5
), the first term tends to zero by Lemma
5.2
as
n
tends to infinity. Since
1-2^{-(\tau-1)}>1/2
for
\tau>2
, for
n
sufficiently large (depending also on the constant
m_{w}
) it follows that

\mathbb{P}\big(\neg\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\cap\{w_{0}\in[m_{w},2m_{w})\}\big)\big)\leq 1-m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})+o(1)\leq 1-m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}/2,

and (
A.8
) follows. We proceed to (
A.9
).
Conditionally on the realization of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
(present in the conditioning in
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
A.7
)), we define the following set and random variable:

(A.19)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}:=\{u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}):u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\},\quad X:=\mathbbm{1}_{\{0\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}):\\
x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})\end{subarray}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}},

with
\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}
from (
A.10
).
The measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
is a conditional measure where
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}
(defined in (
A.6
)) holds and so

|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|\leq 2\mu_{\tau}(\Lambda_{n}\times[m_{w},2m_{w}))
. Using
\mu_{\tau}
in (
2.2
),
we obtain that deterministically under
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
:

X^{2}\leq 4\big(\mu_{\tau}(\Lambda_{n}\times[m_{w},2m_{w})\big)^{2}\leq 4(m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}n)^{2}.

When
0\in{\mathcal{U}}
holds, then
|{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)|\geq|{\mathcal{U}}|\geq X
, and so we apply Paley-Zygmund’s inequality to
X
under the measure
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
, which yields for
\rho^{\prime}:=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X]/(2n)
that

(A.20)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)|\geq\rho^{\prime}n\big)

\displaystyle\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(|{\mathcal{U}}|\geq\rho^{\prime}n,\ 0\in{\mathcal{U}}\big)\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(X\geq\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X]/2\big)

\displaystyle\geq(1/4)\frac{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X]^{2}}{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X^{2}]}\geq\frac{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X]^{2}}{16n^{2}m_{w}^{-2(\tau-1)}}.

We now bound the numerator on the right-hand side from below.
Conditionally on
|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|
, the vertices have a uniform location in
\Lambda_{n}
, so

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})\mid u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}),|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|\big)=\big(n-C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n}\big)/n\geq 1/2,

where the last inequality follows from assuming that
n
is sufficiently large (recall
k_{n}=\Theta\big(\log^{1/\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}}(n)\big)
by (
A.5
)).
The conditioning on
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime}
implies that
|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|\geq\mu_{\tau}(\Lambda_{n}\times[m_{w},2m_{w}))/2
. Using linearity of expectation of
X
in (
A.19
), and the tower rule, (by first conditioning on
|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|
) we obtain for
n
sufficiently large

(A.21)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big[X\big]

\displaystyle\geq(\mu_{\tau}(\Lambda_{n}\times[m_{w},2m_{w}))/2)

\displaystyle\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\Big[\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})\mid u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}),|\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w})|\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\cdot\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(\{0\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}\cap\{u\overset{\pi}{\longrightarrow}{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\}\mid u\in\Xi_{n}[m_{w},2m_{w}),x_{u}\notin\Lambda(0,C_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}k_{n})\big)

\displaystyle\geq nm_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}(1-2^{-(\tau-1)})(1/2)\cdot(1/2)\cdot q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}\geq nq_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}2^{-3},

where the second bound follows if
m_{w}
is chosen as in Claim
A.2
, and from the definition of
\mu_{\tau}
in (
2.2
); the last bound holds since
2^{-(\tau-1)}\leq 1/2
for
\tau>2
.
Substituting the last bound (
A.21
) in the numerator on the right-hand side to (
A.20
), we have obtained that

\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}\big(|{\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)|\geq\rho^{\prime}n)\geq 2^{-10}q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}^{2},

holds with

\rho^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{bb}}[X]/(2n)\geq q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}2^{-4}
,
which yields the statement of Lemma
A.1
for
\rho_{\ref{lemma:construction}}=\min\{q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}2^{-4},2^{-10}q_{\ref{lemma:lower-conn-bb-2}}^{2}\}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.14
.

We start with the first inequality in (
5.45
).
Using
\widetilde{\mathbb{P}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
in (
A.7
), we observe that the bound in Lemma
A.1
holds uniformly over all realizations of
{\mathcal{G}}_{n,1}
satisfying
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}
. Hence, by first taking expectation over these possible realizations, we obtain that

\mathbb{P}({\mathcal{C}}_{n,2}(0)\mid{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{reg}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{bb}}(n,k_{n}),w_{0}\in[m_{w},2m_{w}])\geq\rho_{\ref{lemma:construction}}

also holds. The statement now follows with
\rho:=\rho_{\ref{lemma:construction}}m_{w}^{-(\tau-1)}/2
by the law of total probability combining (
A.8
) and (
A.9
) of Lemma
A.1
.
The second inequality in (
5.45
) for
n=\infty
follows from the same construction as the greedy path in the proof of Claim
5.5
below (
A.16
), making the greedy path infinitely long. We leave it to the reader to fill in the details.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs using first-moment method

We start with the proof of Claim
6.4
.

Proof of Claim
6.4
.

By Palm theory and symmetry of the PPP
\mu_{\tau}
on
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, using that we conditioned on
0\in\Xi
in the Palm version, we consider
{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)
in (
6.20
) for some
i\geq 1
. We estimate the mean from below using
t_{k}=n_{k}^{1/d}/(2k)

\lambda_{i}:=\mu_{\tau}({\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0))=(2^{i}t_{k})^{d}-(2^{i-1}t_{k})^{d}=(2^{i-1}t_{k})^{d}(2^{d}-1)\geq 2^{d(i-1)}(2k)^{-d}n_{k}\geq 2^{d(i-2)}n_{k}^{\delta}.

Hence, since
|\Xi_{N_{k}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)|
is distributed as
{\mathrm{Poi}}(\lambda_{i})
with
\lambda_{i}>2^{d(i-2)}n_{k}^{\delta}
, by Chernoff bounds for Poisson random variables (see Lemma
C.1
applied with
x=2
, using
1+2\log(2)-2\geq 1/4
),

\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(|\Xi_{N_{k}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)|>2\cdot\mu_{\tau}\big({\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)\big)\big)\leq\exp(-\lambda_{i}(1+2\log 2-2))\leq\exp\big(-2^{d(i-2)-2}n_{k}^{\delta}\big),

and the statement follows for every
c>0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{dense}}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\exists u\in\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w}),i\geq 1:|\Xi_{N_{k}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u})|>2\cdot\mu_{\tau}\big({\mathcal{R}}_{i}(x_{u})\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[|\Xi_{n_{k}}[1,\overline{w})|]\,\cdot\,\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big(\exists i\geq 1:|\Xi_{N_{k}}\cap{\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)|>2\cdot\mu_{\tau}\big({\mathcal{R}}_{i}(0)\big)\big)

\displaystyle\leq n_{k}\sum_{i\geq 1}\exp\big(-2^{d(i-2)-2}n_{k}^{\delta}\big)\leq 2n_{k}\exp\big(-2^{-d-2}n_{k}^{\delta}\big)=o(n_{k}^{-c}).\qed

Now we prove Claim
6.6
used for the upper bound of subexponential decay.

Proof of Claim
6.6
.

For compact sets
{\mathcal{K}}_{1},{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\subseteq{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
, we define
\|{\mathcal{K}}_{1}-{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\|:=\min\{\|x-y\|,x\in{\mathcal{K}}_{1},y\in{\mathcal{K}}_{2}\}
.
We define

\widetilde{t}_{k}:=\|\partial\Lambda_{N}-\partial\Lambda_{n}\|=(N^{1/d}-n^{1/d})/2.

The definition of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}
in (
6.11
) implies that

{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n,N,\overline{w})\subseteq\big\{\exists u\in\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w}),v\in\Xi:\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\geq\widetilde{t}_{k},u\leftrightarrow v\big\},

so that after conditioning on
\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})
it follows by a union bound that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n,N,\overline{w})\big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\Bigg[\sum_{u\in\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})}\mathbb{P}\big(\exists v\in\Xi:\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\geq\tilde{t}_{k},u\leftrightarrow v\mid u\in\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})\big)\Bigg]

(B.1)

\displaystyle=:\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\Bigg[\sum_{u\in\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})}q(u)\Bigg]

Assume
\alpha<\infty
.
Since the diameter of
\Lambda_{n}
is
\sqrt{d}n^{1/d}
, the lower bound on
N
in the statement of Claim
6.6
implies that
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\leq\widetilde{t}_{k}
for all
x_{u},x_{v}\in\Lambda_{n}
. Hence,
v\notin\Xi_{n}
whenever
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|>\widetilde{t}_{k}
. This implies by Markov’s bound, using the connection probability in (
2.3
), and that

w_{u}\leq\overline{w}
and the intensity
\mu_{\tau}
and the translation invariance of the intensity of
\Xi
in (
2.2
), that for all
u\in\Xi_{n}
,

\displaystyle q(u)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\Bigg[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\Xi:\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|>\widetilde{t}_{k}\end{subarray}}p\min\Big\{1,\left(\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}}\right)^{\alpha}\Big\}\Bigg]

(B.2)

\displaystyle=p(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\infty}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\geq\widetilde{t}_{k}}\min\Big\{1,\left(\beta\frac{\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{d}}\right)^{\alpha}\Big\}w_{v}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{v}

\displaystyle=p(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\infty}w_{v}^{-\tau}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|\geq\widetilde{t}_{k},\|x_{v}\|^{d}\leq\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})}\mathrm{d}w_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{v}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+p(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\infty}(\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v}))^{\alpha}w_{v}^{-\tau}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|\geq\widetilde{t}_{k},\|x_{v}\|^{d}\geq\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})}\|x_{v}\|^{-\alpha d}\mathrm{d}w_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{v}

(B.3)

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2},

where we cut the integral into two based on the value on the minimum.
We analyze the two integrals separately. Analyzing
T_{1}
, the integration with respect to
x_{v}
gives the Lebesgue measure of the set
\{x_{v}:\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}\leq\|x_{v}^{d}\|\leq\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})\}
, which is nonzero only if this set is nonempty, and then can be bounded from above by
c_{d}\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})
for some constant depending only on
d
. So we obtain

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq c_{d}p\beta(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}{\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}\leq\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})\}}\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})w_{v}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

\displaystyle=c_{d}p\beta(\tau-1)\Big(\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\overline{w}}\mathbbm{1}{\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}\leq\beta\overline{w}w_{v}^{\sigma}\}}\overline{w}w_{v}^{\sigma-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}+\int_{w_{v}=\overline{w}}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}{\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}\leq\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}w_{v}\}}\overline{w}^{\sigma}w_{v}^{1-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}\Big),

where in the last step we used the definition of
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
in (
1.10
) and cut the integration into two based on the minimum of
\overline{w}
and
w_{v}
.
Since we assumed
\widetilde{t}_{k}\geq\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma+1}
in the statement of the lemma, and
w_{v}^{\sigma}\leq\overline{w}^{\sigma}
, the indicator in the first integral is
0
. Moving the indicator in the second integral into the integration boundary yields

(B.4)

T_{1}\leq c_{d}p\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}(\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}))^{-(\tau-2)}=c_{d}p\beta^{\tau-1}\overline{w}^{\sigma(\tau-1)}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d(\tau-2)}.

We turn to
T_{2}
in (
B.3
). For some
d
-dependent constant
c_{d}^{\prime}
, using again
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
in (
1.10
)

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\infty}(\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v}))^{\alpha}w_{v}^{-\tau}\max\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d},\beta\kappa_{1,\sigma}(\overline{w},w_{v})\}^{1-\alpha}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

(B.5)

\displaystyle=pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=1}^{\overline{w}}(\beta\overline{w}w_{v}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}w_{v}^{-\tau}\max\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d},\beta\overline{w}w_{v}^{\sigma}\}^{1-\alpha}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

(B.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=\overline{w}}^{\infty}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}w_{v})^{\alpha}w_{v}^{-\tau}\max\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d},\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}w_{v}\}^{1-\alpha}\mathrm{d}w_{v}.

For the first integral, we bound
w_{v}^{\sigma}\leq\overline{w}^{\sigma}
, and observe that by the assumption
\widetilde{t}_{k}\geq\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma+1}
in the statement of the lemma, the maximum is always attained at
\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}
. Hence,

(B.7)

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:t2-cut-1}\leq pc_{d}^{\prime}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma+1})^{\alpha}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}.

We split the integral in (
B.6
) according to where the maximum is attained, i.e.,

(
B.6
)

\displaystyle=pc_{d}^{\prime}\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=\max\{\overline{w},\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)\}}^{\infty}w_{v}^{-(\tau-1)}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)\geq\overline{w}\}}pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}\int_{w_{v}=\overline{w}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}w_{v})^{\alpha}w_{v}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

\displaystyle=pc_{d}^{\prime}\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma}(\tau-1)\int_{w_{v}=\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)}^{\infty}w_{v}^{-(\tau-1)}\mathrm{d}w_{v}+pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}\int_{w_{v}=\overline{w}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)}w_{v}^{\alpha-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

(B.8)

\displaystyle=\frac{pc_{d}^{\prime}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\tau-1}(\tau-1)}{\tau-2}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d(\tau-2)}+pc_{d}^{\prime}(\tau-1)\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}\int_{w_{v}=\overline{w}}^{\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)}w_{v}^{\alpha-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}

where the second step follows from the assumption that
\widetilde{t}_{k}\geq\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma+1}
in the statement of the lemma.
For the remaining integral on the right-hand side of (
B.8
), say
T_{22}
, we consider three cases, i.e., for some
C>0

\displaystyle T_{22}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}\big(\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)\big)^{\alpha-(\tau-1)}&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\\
C\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}\log\big(\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)\big)&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\\
C\overline{w}^{\alpha-(\tau-1)}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-(\alpha-1)d}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,\\
\end{cases}

Elementary rewriting of the first and third case yields

\displaystyle T_{22}

\displaystyle\leq\begin{cases}C\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d(\tau-2)}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\tau-1}&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\\
C\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d(\alpha-1)}(\beta\overline{w}^{\sigma})^{\alpha}\log\big(\widetilde{t}_{k}^{d}/(\overline{w}^{\sigma}\beta)\big)&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\\
C\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d(\alpha-1)}\beta^{\alpha}\overline{w}^{\alpha(\sigma+1)-(\tau-1)}&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1.\end{cases}

Combining this bound with (
B.8
), then (
B.7
) and (
B.4
), gives in (
B.3
) and (
B.1
) that for some
C>0,b>0

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\scriptscriptstyle 0}\big({\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{long-edge}}(0,n,N,\overline{w})\big)

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\overline{w}^{b}\widetilde{t}_{k}^{-d\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\}}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha=\tau-1\}}\log(t_{k}))\mathbb{E}[|\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})]|]

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{w}^{b}N^{-d\min\{\alpha-1,\tau-2\}}(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha=\tau-1\}})\log(N))n,

where the last bound follows by the assumed bound in (
6.26
), since
\widetilde{t}_{k}=(N^{1/d}-n^{1/d})/2
, and the intensity of
\Xi_{n}[1,\overline{w})
in (
2.2
). This finishes the proof of (
6.27
) when
\alpha<\infty
.

It remains to show the bound for the case
\alpha=\infty
. In this case, the same calculations hold, with only
T_{1}
in (
B.3
) present, since the connection probability is
0
when the minimum in (
B.2
) is not at
1
.
∎

We proceed with the proofs of two lemmas for the lower bound.

Proof of Lemma
7.3
.

We recall from (
7.31
) that it is sufficient to bound
\mu_{\tau}({\mathbb{R}}^{d+1}_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}})\!=\!\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\big|\big]\!+\!\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big|\big]
.
We introduce some notation: for two functions
g(k),h(k)
, we write
g\!\lesssim\!h
if
g\!=\!O(h)
.
Since
f_{\gamma}(x)
is symmetric around the boundary of
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
(see its definition in (
7.9
)), it is easy to see that
\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\big|\big]\!\leq\!\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big|\big]
. Since
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\!=(d-1)/d
by (
1.9
), for (
7.29
)
it is sufficient to show that

(B.9)

\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big|\big]\lesssim r^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))}+(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\{1-\gamma(\tau-1)=1-1/d\}}\log(r))r^{d-1}.

Using the intensity measure of
\Xi
in (
2.2
), switching to polar coordinates in the first
d
directions, and integrating with respect to the mark-coordinate, we obtain using the exact form of
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|\Xi_{>{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big|\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z=0}^{\infty}(z+r_{k})^{d-1}\int_{f_{\gamma}(z)}^{\infty}(\tau-1)w^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z=0}^{C_{\beta}}(z+r_{k})^{d-1}\mathrm{d}z+\int_{z=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}(z+r_{k})^{d-1}z^{-d\gamma(\tau-1)}\mathrm{d}z

(B.10)

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{z=r_{k}}^{\infty}(z+r_{k})^{d-1}(z^{d}r_{k}^{-d(1-\gamma)})^{-(\tau-1)}\mathrm{d}z=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

The integration length of
I_{1}
is a constant, so
I_{1}\lesssim r_{k}^{d-1}.

For
I_{2}
we apply the binomial theorem, i.e.,

(B.11)

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}r_{k}^{j}\int_{C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}z^{(1-\gamma(\tau-1))d-1-j}\mathrm{d}z.

Analyzing the summands separately, we obtain for
j\leq d-1

r_{k}^{j}\int_{C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}z^{(1-\gamma(\tau-1))d-1-j}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim\begin{cases}r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))}&\text{if }d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))>j,\\
\log(r_{k})r_{k}^{j}&\text{if }d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))=j,\\
r_{k}^{j}&\text{if }d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))<j.\end{cases}

Using these bounds, which are increasing for
j\in[d-1]
, in (
B.11
), we obtain

(B.12)

I_{2}\lesssim(1+\mathbbm{1}_{\{1-\gamma(\tau-1)=1-1/d\}}\log(r_{k}))r_{k}^{d-1}+r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))}.

It remains to bound
I_{3}
in (
B.10
). Using that
\tau>2
by assumption, and
z+r_{k}\leq 2z
,

\displaystyle I_{3}\lesssim r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma)(\tau-1)}\int_{z=r_{k}}^{\infty}z^{-d(\tau-2)-1}\mathrm{d}z\lesssim r_{k}^{d((1-\gamma)(\tau-1)-(\tau-2))}=r_{k}^{d(1-\gamma(\tau-1))}.

Together with the bound on
I_{1}
below the definitions of
I_{1},I_{2}
, and
I_{3}
in (
B.11
), and on
I_{2}
in (
B.12
), this proves (
B.9
) and also finishes the proof of (
7.29
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
7.4
.

We split the expected number of edges depending on the locations of the endpoints of the vertices.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}

\displaystyle\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\Big]=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\Big]

(B.13)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.0pt+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\Big]+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}},\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}\big)\big|\Big]+\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{io}}\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}\big)\big|\Big]

We analyze the first term on the right-hand side and at the end we sketch how the bounds could be adapted for the other three terms.
Since
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)
is measurable with respect to
\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}
, it can be left out of the conditioning. Further, points of
\Xi
in disjoint sets are independently present, hence

(B.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\,\Big|\,\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\big]

We use the notation
g\lesssim h
if
g=O(h)
.
We integrate over the locations and marks of the vertices in
\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}}\cup\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}
by writing
z(x)=\|x-\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}\|
, and upper bound the connectivity function
\mathrm{p}
in (
2.3
) to obtain

(B.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{x_{u}:\|x_{u}\|\leq r_{k}}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|\geq r_{k}}\int_{w_{u}=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z(x_{u}))}\int_{w_{v}=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z(x_{v}))}\frac{(\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{u},w_{v}))^{\alpha}}{\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{\alpha d}}w_{u}^{-\tau}w_{v}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{v}\mathrm{d}w_{u}\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{u}.

We analyze the double integral over the marks.
Define

\displaystyle g_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle:=\int_{w_{1}=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{1})}\int_{w_{2}=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{2})}\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w_{1},w_{2})w_{1}^{-\tau}w_{2}^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}w_{1}\mathrm{d}w_{2},

Using the definition and symmetry of
\kappa_{1,\sigma}
in (
1.10
), we reparametrize by
w\leq\tilde{w}
and using that
f_{\gamma}
is increasing

\displaystyle g_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{w=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{1}\wedge z_{2})}\int_{\widetilde{w}=w}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{1}\vee z_{2})}\kappa_{1,\sigma}(w,\widetilde{w})(w\widetilde{w})^{-\tau}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{w}\mathrm{d}w=\int_{w=1}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{1}\wedge z_{2})}w^{\sigma\alpha-\tau}\int_{\widetilde{w}=w}^{f_{\gamma}(z_{1}\vee z_{2})}\widetilde{w}^{\alpha-\tau}\mathrm{d}\widetilde{w}\mathrm{d}w.

The definition
f_{\gamma}
in (
7.9
) undergoes a change at
z=r_{k}
. When integrating, we have nine cases depending on whether the exponents are below, equal to, or above
-1
each. This yields for
z_{2}\leq\min\{z_{1},r_{k}\}
(so
f_{\gamma}(z_{2})=1\vee(z_{2}/C_{\beta})^{\gamma d}
) that
g_{1}(z_{1},z_{2})\lesssim g(z_{1},z_{2})
,
with

(B.16)

g(z_{1},z_{2}):=\begin{cases}f_{\gamma}(z_{1})^{\alpha-(\tau-1)}z_{2}^{\gamma d(\sigma\alpha-(\tau-1))},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
f_{\gamma}(z_{1})^{\alpha-(\tau-1)}\log(z_{2}),&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
f_{\gamma}(z_{1})^{\alpha-(\tau-1)},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
(1+\log(f_{\gamma}(z_{1})/f_{\gamma}(z_{2})))z_{2}^{\gamma d((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))},&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
\log(f_{\gamma}(z_{1}))\log(z_{2}),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
\log(f_{\gamma}(z_{1})),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
z_{2}^{\gamma d((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))},&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha>2(\tau-1),\\
\log(z_{2}),&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha=2(\tau-1),\\
1,&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha<2(\tau-1).\end{cases}

Returning to (
B.15
), we use
g(z_{1},z_{2})
to bound the inner two integrals from above.
We take a case-distinction on whether the vertex
u
(inside) or
v
(outside) is closer to the boundary
\partial B_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
. Then we obtain (since
f_{\gamma}(x)=1
when
z(x)\leq C_{\beta}
by (
7.9
)),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{x_{u}:\|x_{u}\|\leq r_{k}-C_{\beta}}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|>2r_{k}}\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{-\alpha d}g(z(x_{u}),z(x_{v}))\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{u}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{x_{u}:\|x_{u}\|\leq r_{k}-C_{\beta}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{v}:z(x_{v})\leq z(x_{u})\end{subarray}}\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{-\alpha d}g(z(x_{u}),z(x_{v}))\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{u}

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{x_{v}:C_{\beta}\leq\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}\leq r_{k}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{u}:z(x_{u})\leq z(x_{v})\end{subarray}}\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|^{-\alpha d}g(z(x_{u}),z(x_{v}))\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{u}

(B.17)

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2a}+I_{2b}=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

To evaluate the integrals we change variables. For
I_{1}
we use
z(x_{u})\leq r_{k}
, and
g
is increasing in both its arguments, and that
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\geq|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|\geq z(x_{v}):=t
and use polar coordinates in the second row below. Then
\|x_{v}\|=t+r_{k}
. Thus, since there are
\Theta((t+r_{k})^{d-1})
points outside at distance
t
from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
,

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{x_{u}:\|x_{u}\|\leq r_{k}-C_{\beta}}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|>2r_{k}}|\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}|^{-\alpha d}g(r_{k},z(x_{v}))\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}x_{u}

(B.18)

\displaystyle\lesssim r_{k}^{d}\int_{t>r_{k}}(t+r_{k})^{d-1}t^{-\alpha d}g(r_{k},t)\mathrm{d}t\lesssim r_{k}^{d}\int_{t\geq r_{k}}t^{-d(\alpha-1)-1}g(r_{k},t)\mathrm{d}t.

Before substituting the definition of
g
into the bound, we recall that
f_{\gamma}(t)=C_{\beta}^{-\gamma d}t^{d}r_{k}^{-d(1-\gamma)}
for
t>r_{k}
by (
7.9
). The following elementary integration inequalities will be helpful soon:

(B.19)

\displaystyle r_{k}^{d}

\displaystyle\int_{t\geq r_{k}}\!t^{-d(\alpha-1)-1}f_{\gamma}(t)^{\alpha-(\tau-1)}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim r_{k}^{d-d(1-\gamma)(\alpha-(\tau-1))}\int_{t\geq r_{k}}\!\!\!t^{-d(\tau-2)-1}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma(\alpha-(\tau-1)))},

(B.20)

\displaystyle r_{k}^{d}

\displaystyle\int_{t\geq r_{k}}t^{-d(\alpha-1)-1}\mathrm{d}t\lesssim r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha)}.

Substituting the definition of
g
in (
B.16
) into (
B.18
), since
r_{k}<t
, we must set
z_{2}=r_{k}
in
g
in (
B.16
). We obtain then by elementary integration on (
B.18
) and the bounds in (
B.19
)-(
B.20
) that:

(B.21)

I_{1}\lesssim\begin{cases}r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)))},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma(\alpha-(\tau-1)))}\log(r_{k}),&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma(\alpha-(\tau-1)))},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))},&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha)}\log^{2}(r_{k}),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha)}\log(r_{k}),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)))},&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha>2(\tau-1),\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha)}\log(r_{k}),&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha=2(\tau-1),\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha)},&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha<2(\tau-1).\end{cases}

We turn to
I_{2a}
in (
B.17
), handling the case when the outside vertex
v
is closer to the boundary
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
than
u
, implying
z(x_{v})\leq z(x_{u})
. We reparametrise this integral based on the distance
z_{u}
from
\partial{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
of the inside vertex
u
. Indeed, when
z_{u}\in[C_{\beta},r_{k}]
then
\|x_{u}\|=r_{k}-z_{u}
. Since
v
is closer, we must also have that
z_{v}=\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}\in[C_{\beta},z_{u}]
, and hence
t:=\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|\in[z_{u}+C_{\beta},2r_{k}]
. Hence,

\displaystyle I_{2a}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}\int_{x_{u}:r_{k}-\|x_{u}\|=z_{u}}\int_{t=z_{u}+C_{\beta}}^{2r_{k}}\int_{z_{v}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{u}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{v}:z(x_{v})=z_{v},\\
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|=t\end{subarray}}t^{-\alpha d}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x_{u}\mathrm{d}z_{u}.

The integrand does not depend on
x_{v}
anymore, hence the most inside integral, over
x_{v}
, can be bounded from above by maximizing the Lebesgue measure of where
x_{v}
may fall:
x_{v}
has distance
t
from
x_{u}
and distance
z_{v}
from the boundary. Some geometry shows that
x_{v}
is then on the intersection of two spheres with radii
t
and
r_{k}+z_{v}
, respectively, with Lebesgue measure then at most
\Theta(t^{d-2})
. We can also integrate over all the potential locations
x_{u}
, giving a factor
\Theta((r_{k}-z_{u})^{d-1})
, so we obtain

\displaystyle I_{2a}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}(r_{k}-z_{u})^{d-1}\int_{t=z_{u}+C_{\beta}}^{2r_{k}}t^{d-2-\alpha d}\int_{z_{v}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{u}}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}z_{u}

(B.22)

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}(r_{k}-z_{u})^{d-1}z_{u}^{-d(\alpha-1)-1}\int_{z=C_{\beta}}^{z_{u}}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}z_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{u},

where we integrated over
t
to obtain the second row.
Treating
I_{2b}
in (
B.17
) is very similar, but now we reparametrize the integral based on the distance
z_{v}
of
v
from the boundary and the distance
t=\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|
. We obtain

\displaystyle I_{2b}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z_{v}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}\int_{x_{v}:\|x_{v}\|-r_{k}=z_{v}}\int_{t=z_{v}+C_{\beta}}^{3r_{k}}\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{v}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{u}:z_{u}=z(x_{u}),\\
\|x_{u}-x_{v}\|=t\end{subarray}}t^{-\alpha d}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}x_{u}\mathrm{d}z_{u}\mathrm{d}t\mathrm{d}x_{v}\mathrm{d}z_{v}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{z_{v}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}(r_{k}+z_{v})^{d-1}z_{v}^{-d(\alpha-1)-1}\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{v}}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}z_{u}\mathrm{d}z_{v}.

This bound dominates the bound on
I_{2a}
in (
B.22
). Applying the binomial theorem on
(r_{k}+z_{v})^{d-1}
, we obtain

(B.23)

\displaystyle I_{2}=I_{2a}+I_{2b}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}r_{k}^{j}\int_{z_{v}=C_{\beta}}^{r_{k}}z_{v}^{d(2-\alpha)-2-j}\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{v}}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}z_{u}\mathrm{d}z_{v}.

We evaluate the inner integral using the definition of
g
in (
B.16
), and since
z_{u}\leq z_{v}
we set
z_{1}=z_{v},z_{2}=z_{u}
in (
B.16
), and
f_{\gamma}(z)=(z/C_{\beta})^{\gamma d}
, and obtain the nine cases:

(B.24)

\displaystyle\int_{z_{u}=C_{\beta}}^{z_{v}}g(z_{u},z_{v})\mathrm{d}z_{u}\lesssim\begin{cases}z_{v}^{\gamma d((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))+1},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
z_{v}^{\gamma d(\alpha-(\tau-1))+1}\log(z_{v}),&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
z_{v}^{\gamma d(\alpha-(\tau-1))+1},&\text{if }\alpha>\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
z_{v}^{\gamma d((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))+1},&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha>\tau-1,\\
z_{v}\log^{2}(z_{v}),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha=\tau-1,\\
z_{v}\log(z_{v}),&\text{if }\alpha=\tau-1,\sigma\alpha<\tau-1,\\
z_{v}^{\gamma d((\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1))+1},&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha>2(\tau-1),\\
z_{v}\log(z_{v}),&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha=2(\tau-1),\\
z_{v},&\text{if }\alpha<\tau-1,(\sigma+1)\alpha<2(\tau-1).\end{cases}

For
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{0,\alpha-(\tau-1),(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\}
, recall that
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})
counts the multiplicity of the maximum in
{\mathcal{Y}}
. Substituting (
B.24
) into (
B.23
), the nine cases can be summarized as obtaining the integrand of
z_{v}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))-1-j}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})-1}(z_{v})
, so that following the similar reasoning as from (
B.11
) to (
B.12
),

\displaystyle I_{2}\lesssim\begin{cases}r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})-1}(r_{k}),&\text{if }d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})))>d-1,\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})}(r_{k}),&\text{if }d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})))=d-1,\\
r_{k}^{d-1},&\text{if }d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}})))<d-1,\end{cases}

where the second bound follows from similar reasoning as in (
B.11
) leading to (
B.12
).
The presence of a
(d-1)
term and the additional
\log
-factors ensure that the bound on
I_{2}
dominates the bound on
I_{1}
in (
B.21
). Recalling that
I_{1}+I_{2}
dominates the expected number of edges below the
\gamma
-suppressed profile from (
B.17
), this yields by (
B.14
) and
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}=(d-1)/d
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[

\displaystyle\big|{\mathcal{E}}\big(\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{in}},\Xi_{\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{\gamma}\setminus{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}}^{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{out}}\big)\big|\,\Big|\,\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}}\cup\Xi_{{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}},{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}(\eta)\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\begin{cases}r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})-1}(r_{k}),&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))>\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
r_{k}^{d(2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))}\log^{\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})}(r_{k}),&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}},\\
r_{k}^{d-1},&\text{if }2-\alpha+\gamma\max({\mathcal{Y}}))<\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}.\end{cases}

To obtain bounds on the other three expectations in (
B.13
), one can
replace the integrals over the marks in (
B.15
) by summing over the mark intervals
I_{j}
defined in (
7.15
): the upper bounds on the number of vertices using the definitions of
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{in})}(\eta)
and
{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathrm{regular}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k,\mathrm{out})}(\eta)
in (
7.17
) ensure that the total number of points in each interval only differs from its expectation by a constant factor. Then one can use an upper bound on the mark of each vertex in
I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}}
in (
7.16
), given by
I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}}=[w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{loc}})/2^{j},w_{\eta}(kc_{\mathrm{loc}})/2^{j-1})
, and thus also the mark is at most a factor two larger than the mark of a typical vertex in
I_{j}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{loc}}}
. Lastly, the distance between vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{in}}
and outside
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
(but within distance
r_{k}
of
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
) can be bounded from below by
r_{k}/2
by Lemma
A.1
, and analogously we can bound the distance between vertices in
{\mathcal{R}}_{\mathrm{out}}
and vertices inside
{\mathcal{B}}_{kM_{\mathrm{in}}}
.
We leave it to the reader to fill in the details.
∎

Appendix C
Auxiliary proofs

It remains to prove Claim
7.5
.

Proof of Claim
7.5
.

We first assume
\alpha<\infty
.
We set
\gamma:=\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}
defined in (
7.13
) in Lemmas
7.3
and
7.4
, and compare the exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
on the right-hand sides of (
7.29
) and (
7.30
), respectively. We will show that the three cases there can be ‘merged’ by taking the exponent of
r_{k}^{d}=\Theta(k)
to be
\max({\mathcal{Z}})
, and the exponent of the log-factors in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) is then at most
\mathfrak{m}_{\mathcal{Z}}-1
. We distinguish whether
(\star)
in (
7.13
) holds with equality or inequality. Let
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{0,\alpha-(\tau-1),(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\}
as defined above (
7.13
) and
{\mathcal{Z}}=\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
as usual.

Case 1A. Assume
1-\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}(\tau-1)>2-\alpha+\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}\max({\mathcal{Y}})
, and
\alpha<\infty
.

This, by (
7.13
) implies that
\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}=1/(\sigma+1)
.
Considering that
{\mathcal{Y}}=\{0,\alpha-(\tau-1),(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)\}
, the assumed inequality of Case 1A turns then into the following three inequalities after elementary rearrangements:

(C.1)

\displaystyle(\sigma+1)(\alpha-1)>\tau-1,\qquad\qquad\alpha<(\sigma+1)(\alpha-1),\qquad(\sigma+1)\alpha-(\tau-1)<(\sigma+1)(\alpha-1).

Solving the last inequality yields
\sigma<\tau-2
, which readily implies
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}<0
by its definition in (
1.12
). The second inequality implies that
\alpha>1+1/\sigma>1+1/(\tau-2)
, which is equivalent to
\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}}<0
. It is left to show that
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha<0
. The first inequality, and
(\sigma+1)/(\tau-1)<1
(coming from the third inequality), yield

\frac{1}{\alpha-1}<\frac{\sigma+1}{\tau-1}<1,

which is equivalent to
\alpha>2
.
Summarizing, we obtained that under the assumption of Case 1A,
\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}<0\leq\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
, hence
\max({\mathcal{Z}})=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
and
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Z}})=1
.
We also check that in this case the exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) are respectively at most
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}=(d-1)/d\geq 0
. This is true since

2-\alpha+\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}\max({\mathcal{Y}})<1-\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}(\tau-1)=1-\frac{\tau-1}{\sigma+1}<0,\qquad

where the first inequality follows by the assumption of Case 1A, and the second one by the last inequality in (
C.1
). Hence, under Case 1A, the third case holds in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
), the exponent of
r_{k}^{d}
is
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}=\max({\mathcal{Z}})
in this case, and there are no logarithmic factors, which is also reflected in
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Z}})-1=0
. Hence, both inequalities in the statement of (
7.33
) hold in Case 1A.

Case 1B) Assume
\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}=1-\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}(\tau-1)=2-\alpha+\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}\max({\mathcal{Y}})
and
\alpha<\infty
.

The exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) match by assumption, and the three cases can be summarized as this exponent being
\max\{\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
.
By the equality assumption for Case 1B, we compute that

(C.2)

\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}=\frac{\alpha-1}{\max({\mathcal{Y}})+\tau-1}=\min\Big\{\frac{\alpha-1}{\tau-1},\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha},\frac{\alpha-1}{(\sigma+1)\alpha-(\tau-1)}\Big\}.

Since
\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}\leq 1/(\sigma+1)
by (
7.13
), it is elementary to verify that the third term may be minimal only when
\tau\leq 2+\sigma
.

Assume
\tau\leq 2+\sigma
.

Using (
C.2
), (
1.7
), (
1.8
), (
1.12
), it is elementary to verify that

(C.3)

\zeta_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}=1-\min\Big\{\frac{\alpha-1}{\tau-1},\frac{\alpha-1}{\alpha},\frac{\alpha-1}{(\sigma+1)\alpha-(\tau-1)}\Big\}(\tau-1)=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}.

It immediately follows that
\max\{\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}=\max({\mathcal{Z}})
. Hence, the exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) equal to
\max({\mathcal{Z}})
, which is what we aimed for in (
7.33
).
We now treat the exponent of the logarithm in (
7.30
) and in (
7.29
). Recall that
\mathfrak{m}
in (
2.5
) denotes the multiplicity of the maximum of a set. From (
C.2
) and (
C.3
) it follows that
\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})=\mathfrak{m}(\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\})
. Hence, using that
\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}\}
,

(C.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Z}})=\mathfrak{m}(\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\})=\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}>\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}}+(\mathfrak{m}({\mathcal{Y}})+1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}=\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}}+\mathbbm{1}_{\{\zeta_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}<\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}}

The relations
between
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
and
1-\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}(\tau-1)
on the one hand, and
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
and
2-\alpha+\gamma_{\neg\mathrm{nn}}\max({\mathcal{Y}})
on the other hand, are the same by our equality assumption in Case 1B. This confirms that the log-factor in (
7.29
) is at most the log-factor in (
7.30
), showing therefore the first inequality of (
7.33
), and that the log-factor in (
7.30
) equals what we aimed for in the second inequality of (
7.33
), thereby finishing Case 1B when
\tau\leq 2+\sigma
.

Assume
\tau>2+\sigma
.

The minimum in (
C.2
) is never attained at the third term (and equivalently the maximum in
{\mathcal{Y}}
is never attained at
(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)
).
Similarly to (
C.3
), we obtain that
\zeta_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}}\}
. Moreover,
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}<0\leq\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}
by the formula for
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
in (
1.12
), yielding
\max({\mathcal{Z}})=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{\neg nn}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
. Hence, the exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
in (
7.29
) and (
7.30
) equal to
\max({\mathcal{Z}})
, which is what we aimed for in (
7.33
).
For the log-factor we argue similarly as in (
C.4
) and below, with the additional restriction that the maximum in
{\mathcal{Y}}
is never attained at
(\sigma+1)\alpha-2(\tau-1)
, and the maximum in
{\mathcal{Z}}
is never attained at
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
.

Case 2. Assume
\alpha=\infty
.

Since
\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}}=2-\alpha=-\infty
,
\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}}=-(\tau-2)<0
(
\tau>2
by assumption in Lemma
7.2
), and
\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}=(d-1)/d\geq 0
, we obtain
\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{ll}},\zeta_{\mathrm{lh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}=\max\{\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}},\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}=\max\{1-\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}(\tau-1),\zeta_{\mathrm{nn}}\}
by the formula of
\zeta_{\mathrm{hh}}
in (
1.12
).
Since
\gamma_{\mathrm{hh}}=\gamma_{\mathrm{\neg nn}}
when
\alpha=\infty
, it follows that the exponents of
r_{k}^{d}
and
\log(r_{k})
in (
7.29
) and (
7.34
) coincide. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lastly, we state a Poisson concentration bound (without proof) that we often rely on in the paper.

Lemma C.1

(Poisson bound
[
57
]
)
.

For
x>1
,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(\lambda)\geq x\lambda\big)\leq\exp(-\lambda(1+x\log(x)-x)),

and for
x<1
,

\mathbb{P}\big({\mathrm{Poi}}(\lambda)\leq x\lambda\big)\leq\exp(-\lambda(1-x-x\log(1/x)).
